# `lcaisalane` -- PASS 1 ON THE DESIGN: the LCA sparse-I/O byte-lane handler ends the host on an ordinary byte access to two documented UART registers and hands the guest a silent zero for a third

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this brief's author stated
wrongly.  **Check the premises, not just the derivations.**  Where I state something as
fact, ask whether it is true.  A seat that re-derives a premise from something this brief
did NOT give it is worth more than a seat that agrees.

**THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`.**  There are THREE pristine-2021-upstream
copies of GXemul under the project root (`./src/` and two under
`_archive/audit-results-*/builds/`).  They are NOT this fork.  If your line numbers
disagree with mine by hundreds you are reading one of them — say so rather than
reconciling.

**If you cannot read files that is expected** — every fact you need is inlined.  Name any
premise you cannot settle from this packet rather than guessing at it.

**Where this brief states an ARITHMETIC claim — an index range, an offset, a count of
reachable addresses — ENUMERATE it rather than reasoning about it.**  Walk the values.
A derivation that skips one masking line reads exactly like a correct one.

**And ENUMERATE THE RIGHT DOMAIN — a domain is itself a premise.**  Ask what the PRODUCER of
these values can actually emit before you walk them.  Walking every member of a set you have
not audited is not enumeration; it is a derivation with more steps, and it reads exactly like
a correct one.

**VERDICT LINE FIRST**, so your answer survives truncation.

Ordinary bounds-correctness engineering on a bus-bridge byte-lane handler.  Prose only; you may SKETCH an
edit against the full function inlined below (that is why it is inlined), but nothing here is applied.

## §C0 — HAS THIS ROUND SHIPPED?  NO.

## §C — SETTLED PREMISES (re-inlined; say which you cannot settle from this packet)
S1. `fatal()` RETURNS (`src/core/debugmsg.c`, grep `void fatal(`: no early-out, no exit), so every `fatal()`
    here is a diagnostic and the process exit is the separate `exit(1)` line.
S2. THE ROW (filed 2026-09-08, assigned the `ofs > len` exit on the 2026-09-09 adjudication after a double
    count with `lcabadval`): `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)` is the LAST executable process exit in `dev_lca.c`
    once #516 (the two interrupt stubs) and #518 (`lca_ioc`'s six value guards and its default arm) land.
S3. THE OPTION SET for a de-fatalisation, all seven members: 1 delete; 2 silent decline; 3 decline + latched
    announce (the house idiom: N = 1 per device instance, `(once)` in the text); 4 accept; 5 FAULT -- NOT
    AVAILABLE ON ALPHA, `memory_rw.c` dispatches a failed-device exception only for MIPS and M88K; 6 backed
    accept; 7 implement.  Codex's design reading (`regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/codex_design_20260910.txt`)
    recommends member 3 with `ofs >= len`, a `return 0` for BOTH directions on a refused lane, and checking
    the downstream read's result BEFORE the packing loop.  That recommendation is a CANDIDATE here, not a
    ruling.
S4. The emulated Alpha DECODES THE BWX BYTE/WORD LOADS AND STORES WITHOUT ANY CPU-TYPE GATE:
    `src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr.c`, grep `case 0x0a: loadstore_type = 0; load = 1;` (`ldbu`), `case 0x0c:`
    (`ldwu`), `case 0x0e:` (`stb`).  So a guest instruction reaches this handler with `len == 1` and
    `len == 2` even though a real 21066 has no BWX.  (`regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py` already drives `stb`
    on this machine.)
S5. The handler is registered by `dev_lca.c` (grep `memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_isa"`)
    on `alphabook1` only (`device_add(machine, "lca")` under `ST_ALPHABOOK1`); the access arrives through
    `memory_rw` with `len` in {1, 2, 4, 8} and `relative_addr` any byte offset inside the window.
S6. THE HARDWARE, from the local manual `_scratchpad/21066_66a_hrm.txt` (Alpha 21066/21066A HRM), which is
    where the fork's `>> 5` comes from: S6.2.6 (`:8840`) maps CPU physical address bits <31:7> to PCI
    address bits <26:2>; the byte-enable table is Table 6-3 at `:8857`, and `:8850` says "Physical address
    bits <4:3> also specify transfer size".  Verbatim rows of that table (extracted text; the header is
    staggered by the extractor, the DATA rows are not):

        a<6:5> a<4:3> Size      c_be_l<3:0>   PCI Memory ad<1:0>   PCI I/O ad<1:0>   Type
        00     00     Byte      1110          00                   00                Masked
        01     00     Byte      1011 / 0111   00                   01                Masked
        10     00     Byte                    00                   10                Masked
        11     00     Byte                    00                   11                Masked
        00     01     Word      1100 / 1001   00                   00                Masked
        01     01     Word      0011          00                   01                Masked
        10     01     Word                    00                   10                Masked
        00     10     Tribyte   1000 / 0001   00                   00                Masked
        01     10     Tribyte                 00                   01                Masked

    So on hardware the LANE is a<6:5> (it becomes PCI I/O ad<1:0>) and the SIZE is a<4:3>; the CPU issues
    a LONGWORD or QUADWORD access whatever the size encoded.  THE MODEL: `relative_addr >>= 5` keeps a<6:5>
    as the two low bits of the derived port (so `ofs = port & 3` IS the hardware lane -- the witness probe
    proves the lane is a function of the port, not an independent field), DISCARDS a<4:3> entirely, and uses
    the CPU access width `len` as the size.  That divergence is a premise question below, not a settled fact.

## THE FULL ENCLOSING FUNCTION (verbatim from the working tree; the whole handler, every exit path)
```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
{
	unsigned int ofs, i;
	uint8_t byte;

	relative_addr >>= 5;

	ofs = relative_addr & 3;
	if (ofs > len) {
		fatal("[ ofs=%i len=%i in lca_isa access function. "
		    "aborting ]\n", ofs, len);
		exit(1);
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
		byte = data[ofs % len];
		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
	}

	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);

	for (i=0; i<len; i++)
		data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;

	return 1;
}
```
`LCA_ISA_BASE` is `(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x10000000)`.  There is no common tail after the read loop other than
`return 1`; the write arm returns the downstream result directly.

## THE WITNESS (rung 3, Opus measuring seat, `regress/lca_isalane_probe.py`, unmodified `alphabook1`, no
## disk, no OS; `regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/witness_20260910/HEAD_run1.out` and `HEAD_run2.out`, both
## `LCAISALANE_RESULT=13/16`, sentinel `LCAISALANE_DONE`)
Guest LDBU/LDL/STB instructions through the sparse window, with the UART at ISA 0x3f8 (lane = port & 3):
```
port & 3 == 0   ->  ofs 0.  data[0] = byte.                                         CORRECT   (`live': 8042 data 0x55)
port & 3 == 1   ->  ofs 1.  `1 > 1' is FALSE, so ACCEPTED -- and the packing loop
                    `for (i = 0; i < 1; i++) data[i] = (i == 1)? byte : 0' is never
                    true, so THE DEVICE BYTE IS DISCARDED AND THE GUEST IS HANDED ZERO
                    with a success return.  A false success, not a decline.            `ofs1 zero': 0x0, want 0x60 (LSR)
port & 3 == 2   ->  ofs 2.  `2 > 1' -> fatal() + exit(1).                            `ofs2 exit': dead     (MSR 0x3fe)
port & 3 == 3   ->  ofs 3.  `3 > 1' -> fatal() + exit(1).                            `ofs3 exit': dead     (scratch 0x3ff)
```
Controls green on HEAD: `base alive`, `live` (lane 0 returns the keyboard controller's reply), `ofs1 devsig`
and `ofs1 wide` (the SAME LSR read through the flat mapping and through a 32-bit LDL at lane 1 both return
the LSR's fixed bits 0x60 -- so the device answers and RAM would not), `ctl`/`ctl zero` (a lane-0 read of a
port with no device), `ofs2 devsig`/`ofs2 wide`, `ofs3 devsig`/`ofs3 wide` (each register's value through the
other two paths), and the two `attrib` rows (the death is attributed by the handler's own `[ ofs=%i len=%i in
lca_isa access function. aborting ]` text).  Three RED rows, all the defect.

## WHAT CODEX'S DESIGN READING SAID (candidate, not ruling; its verdict line verbatim)
*"VERDICT: Use `ofs >= len` with member 3, and return downstream failure before packing the byte. S3's length
census is incomplete in this tree; the uninitialized-byte claim is conditional."*  Its sketch: keep the decode;
clear the caller's buffer for reads only; reject `ofs >= len` through a per-instance latch and `return 0`;
keep the write arm's immediate downstream return (indexing may become `data[ofs]`); check the downstream READ's
result immediately and `return 0` on failure BEFORE the packing loop; only successful reads reach the loop and
`return 1`.  It also found: both downstream `memory_rw` calls lack `PHYSICAL` though they target registered
physical ISA addresses; the old diagnostic prints `size_t len` with `%i`; changing only `>` to `>=` while
keeping the exit would EXPAND termination to the equality cases (the obvious one-character fix makes it worse).

## §E — THE QUESTIONS (verdict line first; cite by grep anchor, never by line number)
1. **PREMISES AS QUESTIONS.**
   (a) Is the model's lane decode the hardware's?  From Table 6-3, a<6:5> -> PCI I/O ad<1:0>; the model's
       `(relative_addr >> 5) & 3` is a<6:5>.  Confirm or refute by walking one address: a guest LDL at sparse
       offset `(0x3fd << 5)` -- which lane, which port, which PCI ad<1:0>?
   (b) The model DISCARDS a<4:3> (the hardware size) and uses `len`.  For a real 21066 driver -- which has no
       BWX and issues LDL with the size in a<4:3> -- does the model serve a byte read of the LSR correctly
       (LDL, a<6:5> = 01, a<4:3> = 00 -> len 4, ofs 1 -> `data[1] = byte`)?  Which hardware-legal encodings
       does the model serve WRONGLY today (a word at lane 3? a tribyte?), and is any of that THIS round's
       scope or a separate row (my premise: separate -- `lcaisasize`, filed if you agree it is real)?
   (c) Given S4, can a guest binary on the emulated machine issue `ldbu`/`ldwu`/`stb` at all, or is the
       len-1/len-2 path reachable only from the debugger?  My premise: yes, the decoder has no gate, and
       the witness DID issue LDBU from guest code.  If you disagree, say what would stop it.
   (d) What does a DEVICE_ACCESS `return 0` do to the guest on ALPHA?  Codex says the destination register is
       left UNCHANGED (no fault path).  Read `src/cpus/memory_rw.c` around the device dispatch (grep
       `mem->devices[i].f(`) and the Alpha load template (`cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c`): what value, if
       any, lands in the register, and does the CPU keep running?  This decides whether "decline" is even
       observable to a guest and therefore what the detector's channel is.
   (e) The `#i` cases: for `len == 2` and `ofs == 3`, the write arm today does `data[3 % 2] = data[1]` -- is
       that the tribyte/word lane semantics or nonsense?  After `>=` it is declined.  Enumerate every
       (len, ofs) cell -- len in {1,2,4,8}, ofs in {0,1,2,3} -- and label each: served correctly today /
       false success today / exit today / declined after the candidate / still served after the candidate.
       That table IS the (d) DEPENDS-ON enumeration for the detector; a cell you cannot label is a premise
       I have not settled.
2. **THE SMALLEST EDIT that breaks the candidate fix and still passes the rows you would expect** -- name the
   row you expect to stay green.  Candidates I already see: `>=` back to `>` (the equality cells); `return 0`
   moved inside the not-yet-warned branch (the second refused access forwards); the failed-read `return 0`
   changed to `return 1`; the failure check moved below the packing loop; the latch shared with one of the
   eight `LCA_W_IOC_*` bits #518 added; `PHYSICAL` dropped again.  Name the ones I have not.
3. **OPTION-SET COMPLETENESS.**  Is there a member S3 did not name for THIS site?  In particular: is member 4
   (ACCEPT) available for the `ofs == len` equality cells -- i.e. does the packing loop, once fixed to place
   the byte, make those accesses CORRECT rather than refusable?  Is member 7 (honour a<4:3>) the right
   disposition for (b) and what would it cost?  Grep the fork for the sibling idiom: `lca_ioc`'s #518
   default arm and `lca_pci_conf` in the same file.
4. **THE DETECTOR SHAPE the fix must ship with** -- rows, not prose.  From the (d) table in 1(e): an accept
   row per served cell (the de-escalation clause: the old exit was the tripwire for lane-guard growth), a
   decline row per refused cell reading the DECLINE CHANNEL (the zero downstream call, the unchanged
   register, the once-latched text), the (b) reinstatement, the two failure-propagation rows codex named, and
   `latch indep` against a #518 bit.  Which of these is VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION -- a row whose channel cannot
   vary under its mutant -- given your answer to 1(d)?
5. **If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE — say so.**

Do not run builds or probes (three kill tables are using the scratch clones and `build/`); do not propose a
patch to apply -- a sketch against the function above is welcome.

/*

 *
 *  Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without
 *  modification, are permitted provided that the following conditions are met:
 *
 *  1. Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright
 *     notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.
 *  2. Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright
 *     notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer in the
 *     documentation and/or other materials provided with the distribution.
 *  3. The name of the author may not be used to endorse or promote products
 *     derived from this software without specific prior written permission.
 *
 *  THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE AUTHOR AND CONTRIBUTORS ``AS IS'' AND
 *  ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, THE
 *  IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE
 *  ARE DISCLAIMED.  IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE
 *  FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL, SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR CONSEQUENTIAL
 *  DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF SUBSTITUTE GOODS
 *  OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS INTERRUPTION)
 *  HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN CONTRACT, STRICT
 *  LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE) ARISING IN ANY WAY
 *  OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF
 *  SUCH DAMAGE.
 *
 *
 *  Alpha load/store instructions.  (Included from cpu_alpha_instr_inc.c.)
 *
 *
 *  Load/store instructions have the following arguments:
 *  
 *  arg[0] = pointer to the register to load to or store from (uint64_t)
 *  arg[1] = pointer to the base register (uint64_t)
 *  arg[2] = offset (as an int32_t)
 *
 *  NOTE:
 *	Alpha byte and word loads (8- and 16-bit) are unsigned, while
 *	32-bit long words are sign-extended up to 64 bits during a load!
 */


#ifndef LS_IGNORE_OFFSET
static void LS_GENERIC_N(struct cpu *cpu, struct alpha_instr_call *ic)
{
#ifdef LS_B
	unsigned char data[1];
#endif
#ifdef LS_W
	unsigned char data[2];
#endif
#ifdef LS_L
	unsigned char data[4];
#endif
#ifdef LS_Q
	unsigned char data[8];
#endif
	uint64_t addr = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[1]);
	uint64_t data_x;

	addr += (int32_t)ic->arg[2];
#ifdef LS_UNALIGNED
	addr &= ~7;
#endif

#ifdef LS_LOAD
	/*  Load:  */
	if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, data, sizeof(data),
	    MEM_READ, CACHE_DATA)) {
		/*  #215: (Codex/Fable) memory_rw() returned 0 = the translator
		    already signalled a fault (cf. #194's no-translation return);
		    abandon the access instead of exit()ing the host.  */
		return;
	}

	data_x = data[0];
#ifndef LS_B
	data_x += (data[1] << 8);
#ifndef LS_W
	data_x += (data[2] << 16);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[3] << 24);
#ifdef LS_L
	data_x = (int64_t)(int32_t)data_x;
#endif
#ifndef LS_L
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[4] << 32);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[5] << 40);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[6] << 48);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[7] << 56);
#endif
#endif
#endif
	*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = data_x;
#else
	/*  Store:  */
	data_x = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
	data[0] = data_x;
#ifndef LS_B
	data[1] = data_x >> 8;
#ifndef LS_W
	data[2] = data_x >> 16;
	data[3] = data_x >> 24;
#ifndef LS_L
	data[4] = data_x >> 32;
	data[5] = data_x >> 40;
	data[6] = data_x >> 48;
	data[7] = data_x >> 56;
#endif
#endif
#endif

	if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, data, sizeof(data),
	    MEM_WRITE, CACHE_DATA)) {
		/*  #215: (Codex/Fable) memory_rw() returned 0 = the translator
		    already signalled a fault (cf. #194's no-translation return);
		    abandon the access instead of exit()ing the host.  */
		return;
	}

#ifdef LS_LLSC
#ifndef LS_LOAD
	*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = 1;
#endif
#endif

#endif
}
#endif


static void LS_N(struct cpu *cpu, struct alpha_instr_call *ic)
{
	unsigned char *page;
	uint64_t addr = (*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[1]))
#ifndef LS_IGNORE_OFFSET
	    + (int32_t)ic->arg[2]
#endif
	    ;

	const uint32_t mask1 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L1N) - 1;
	const uint32_t mask2 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L2N) - 1;
	const uint32_t mask3 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L3N) - 1;
	uint32_t x1, x2, x3, c;
	struct DYNTRANS_L2_64_TABLE *l2;
	struct DYNTRANS_L3_64_TABLE *l3;
	x1 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N)) & mask1;
	x2 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N)) & mask2;
	x3 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N-DYNTRANS_L3N)) & mask3;
	/*  fatal("X3: addr=%016" PRIx64" x1=%x x2=%x x3=%x\n",
	    (uint64_t) addr, (int) x1, (int) x2, (int) x3);  */
	l2 = cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.l1_64[x1];
	/*  fatal("  l2 = %p\n", l2);  */
	l3 = l2->l3[x2];
	/*  fatal("  l3 = %p\n", l3);  */
#ifdef LS_LOAD
	page = l3->host_load[x3];
#else
	page = l3->host_store[x3];
#endif

#ifdef LS_UNALIGNED
	addr &= ~7;
#endif

#ifdef LS_LLSC
#ifdef LS_LOAD
	/*  TODO: cache-line size!  */
	cpu->cd.alpha.load_linked_addr = addr & ~63;
	cpu->cd.alpha.ll_flag = 1;
#else
	/*  TODO: only invalidate per cache line, not everything!  */
	if (cpu->cd.alpha.ll_flag == 1) {
		int i;
		for (i=0; i<cpu->machine->ncpus; i++)
			cpu->machine->cpus[i]->cd.alpha.ll_flag = 0;
	} else {
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = 0;
		return;
	}
#endif
#endif

	c = addr & 8191;

#ifndef LS_B
	if (c &
#ifdef LS_W
	    1
#endif
#ifdef LS_L
	    3
#endif
#ifdef LS_Q
	    7
#endif
	    ) {
		LS_GENERIC_N(cpu, ic);
		return;
	}
	else
#endif

	if (page != NULL) {
#ifdef LS_LOAD
#ifdef HOST_BIG_ENDIAN
		uint64_t data_x;
		data_x = page[c];
#ifndef LS_B
		data_x += (page[c+1] << 8);
#ifndef LS_W
		data_x += (page[c+2] << 16);
		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+3] << 24);
#ifndef LS_L
		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+4] << 32);
		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+5] << 40);
		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+6] << 48);
		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+7] << 56);
#endif
#endif
#endif
#ifdef LS_L
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = (int64_t)(int32_t)data_x;
#else
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = data_x;
#endif
#else
#ifdef LS_B
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = page[c];
#endif
#ifdef LS_W
		uint16_t d = *((uint16_t *) (page + c));
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = d;
#endif
#ifdef LS_L
		int32_t d = *((int32_t *) (page + c));
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = (int64_t)d;
#endif
#ifdef LS_Q
		uint64_t d = *((uint64_t *) (page + c));
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = d;
#endif
#endif
#else
		/*  Store:  */
#ifdef HOST_BIG_ENDIAN
		uint64_t data_x = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
		page[c] = data_x;
#ifndef LS_B
		page[c+1] = data_x >> 8;
#ifndef LS_W
		page[c+2] = data_x >> 16;
		page[c+3] = data_x >> 24;
#ifndef LS_L
		page[c+4] = data_x >> 32;
		page[c+5] = data_x >> 40;
		page[c+6] = data_x >> 48;
		page[c+7] = data_x >> 56;
#endif
#endif
#endif
#else
		/*  Native byte order:  */
#ifdef LS_B
		page[c] = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
#endif
#ifdef LS_W
		uint32_t d = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
		*((uint16_t *) (page + c)) = d;
#endif
#ifdef LS_L
		uint32_t d = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
		*((uint32_t *) (page + c)) = d;
#endif
#ifdef LS_Q
		uint64_t d = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
		*((uint64_t *) (page + c)) = d;
#endif
#endif

#ifdef LS_LLSC
#ifndef LS_LOAD
		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = 1;
#endif
#endif

#endif	/*  !LS_LOAD  */
	} else
		LS_GENERIC_N(cpu, ic);
}

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "bus_isa.h"
#include "bus_pci.h"
#include "cpu.h"
#include "device.h"
#include "emul.h"
#include "interrupt.h"
#include "machine.h"
#include "memory.h"
#include "misc.h"

#include "thirdparty/alpha_lcareg.h"

#define	LCA_ISA_BASE	(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x10000000)
#define	LCA_ISA_MEMBASE	(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x18000000)


/*  #516: warn-once latch bits, one per stub.  PER-INSTANCE, reached through
    `interrupt->extra' -- see the comment on lca_interrupt_assert().  */
#define	LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT	0	/*  #516  */
#define	LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT	1	/*  #516  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_CONF_CFG1	2	/*  #518: IOC_CONF<1> set -- CFG AD<1:0> = 10/11, not a cycle type  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WMASK0	3	/*  #518: unsupported W_MASK0 encoding declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WMASK1	4	/*  #518: unsupported W_MASK1 encoding declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WEN		5	/*  #518: a DMA window enabled -- translation not modelled  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_DEF_W	6	/*  #518: default-arm write declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_DEF_R	7	/*  #518: default-arm read answered with zero  */

struct lca_data {
	struct pci_data		*pci_data;

	unsigned int		warned;		/*  #516  */

	uint64_t		ioc_conf;
	uint64_t		tlb_enable;
	uint64_t		window_base_0;
	uint64_t		window_mask_0;
	uint64_t		window_t_base_0;
	uint64_t		window_base_1;
	uint64_t		window_mask_1;
	uint64_t		window_t_base_1;
};


/*
 *  lca_interrupt_assert():
 *
 *  Line 0 = ISA interrupt.
 */
void lca_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) interrupt->extra;	/*  #516  */

	/*  #516: this was `fatal(); exit(1)' with NO GUARD -- not a refused value,
	    an unimplemented path -- and DEVINIT below installs it as the ISA
	    interrupt callback that `bus_isa_init' wires to BUS_ISA_IDE0|IDE1.  So an
	    ordinary ISA interrupt ended the host, needing no unusual value anywhere.
	    MEASURED on two independent guest paths: one store to the 8042 command
	    port (regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py, 3/4) and three byte stores to the
	    ns16550 on IRQ 4 (regress/lca_irqstub_uart_probe.py, 2/4).

	    *** THE STUB IS NOT REPLACED BY A FORWARD, BECAUSE A FORWARD WOULD BE A
	    NO-OP.  ALPHA HAS NO INTERRUPT DELIVERY AT ALL. ***  `cd.alpha.irq_asserted'
	    is written by `cpu_alpha.c' and read NOWHERE.  `cpu_dyntrans.c' turns a
	    pending interrupt into a delivered one for FIVE architectures -- ARM and
	    M88K and PPC through their own `irq_asserted', MIPS through the COP0
	    exception model, and SH through `int_to_assert' -- and ALPHA IS ABSENT
	    FROM THAT LIST.  (An earlier draft of this comment said "ARM, M88K and
	    PPC only": that enumeration was made by grepping for `irq_asserted', which
	    is a CORRELATE of delivering rather than the property, and it missed the
	    two architectures that deliver by another mechanism.  The Alpha conclusion
	    is unchanged and is strengthened -- it is the outlier among five, not
	    three.)  Calling `cpu_interrupt()' here would set
	    a field nothing consumes.  Implementing delivery is a SUBSYSTEM -- the
	    dyntrans dispatch, the PALcode entry and the IPL check -- and is its own
	    round, not this one.

	    So the disposition is DECLINE plus a bounded announce, and what it buys is
	    stated narrowly on purpose: THE HOST STOPS DYING.  Interrupts do not start
	    working; they were never delivered on this architecture and still are not.

	    The latch is PER-INSTANCE (`interrupt->extra'), not function-static: a
	    function-static bit would let one guest access to one device instance
	    silence the diagnostic for every other instance, which is a class already
	    filed against this tree.  */
	if (d != NULL && !(d->warned & (1u << LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT))) {	/*  #516  */
		d->warned |= 1u << LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT;
		fatal("[ lca: ISA interrupt assert not implemented (Alpha has no "
		    "interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\n");
	}
}


/*
 *  lca_interrupt_deassert():
 *
 *  Line 0 = ISA interrupt.
 */
void lca_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) interrupt->extra;	/*  #516  */

	/*  #516: the sibling of lca_interrupt_assert() above, same reasoning.  A
	    SEPARATE latch bit, so the two are distinguishable: a driver normally
	    reaches both within one interrupt cycle, and one shared bit would report
	    whichever arrived first and hide the other entirely.  */
	if (d != NULL && !(d->warned & (1u << LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT))) {	/*  #516  */
		d->warned |= 1u << LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT;
		fatal("[ lca: ISA interrupt deassert not implemented (Alpha has no "
		    "interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\n");
	}
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)
{
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	int tag, type1, bus, dev, func, reg;	/*  #515  */
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  1. Decompose the address into a tag.
	 *
	 *  According to NetBSD's lca_pci.c, the address is composed like this:
	 *
	 *	addr = tag << 5 | (regoffset & ~0x03) << 5 | 0x3 << 3
	 */
	reg = (relative_addr >> 5) & 0xfc;
	tag = (relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff;

	/*
	 *  2. Decompose the tag into bus, dev, and func.
	 *
	 *  The tag can be constructed in one of two ways. On the primary
	 *  bus (nr 0):
	 *
	 *	tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (function << 8);
	 *
	 *  and on other busses, the tag is a normal:
	 *
	 *	tag = (bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)
	 */
	/*  printf("tag = 0x%x\n", (int)tag);  */

	/*  #515: `bus' used to be BOTH the configuration CYCLE TYPE and the
	    DESTINATION BUS, and conflating them is the whole defect.  IOC_CONF bit 0
	    selects the cycle type only -- 21066/21066A HRM S6.2.9: "PCI address bits
	    ad<1:0> come directly from configuration cycle type register (IOC_CONF,
	    Section 6.4.2) bits <1:0>" -- and it does NOT carry a bus number.  The
	    destination bus of a type-1 cycle lives in the ADDRESS, per the PCI Local
	    Bus specification's type-1 layout (bus 23..16, dev 15..11, fn 10..8,
	    reg 7..2), which S6.2.9 defers to explicitly.

	    Before this, a guest that merely SET bit 0 -- a value the CONF guard
	    ACCEPTS -- made any subsequent access in this window reach
	    `fatal("TODO. Non-zero bus."); exit(1)'.  MEASURED: two ordinary guest
	    accesses, nothing refused anywhere on the path
	    (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py, 3/4 pre-fix; artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/).  */
	type1 = d->ioc_conf & 1;	/*  #515  */
	bus = 0;			/*  #515  */

	if (!type1) {			/*  #515  */
		/*  #511: was `0x800 << dev'.  `0x800' is a plain int (2^11) and `dev'
		    reaches 20, so the last iteration evaluates 2^31 -- a SIGNED
		    OVERFLOW, undefined by C99 6.5.7p4, not merely
		    implementation-defined.  MEASURED under UBSAN before the change:
		    "dev_lca.c:125:21: runtime error: left shift of 2048 by 20 places
		    cannot be represented in type 'int'", reached by an ordinary guest
		    ldq into the PCI config window (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py against
		    build-asan; artifacts/lcashiftub/ubsan_HEAD_20260907.raw).

		    `0x800U' makes the operand unsigned, so 2048U << 20 is 0x80000000U --
		    inside unsigned int -- and NOTHING ELSE MOVES.

		    *** THE REASON, CORRECTED: AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID `tag' IS "ALREADY A
		    WIDER UNSIGNED QUANTITY".  THAT IS FALSE -- `tag' is a plain signed
		    `int' (:92) -- AND FOUR PASS-2 SEATS CAUGHT IT.  Worse, the claim was
		    backwards: being WIDER is what would CREATE a difference, not remove
		    one.  Had `tag' been `uint64_t', the old `int' 0x80000000 is NEGATIVE
		    and would SIGN-EXTEND to 0xffffffff80000000 for the `&', while
		    `0x800U << 20' zero-extends to 0x0000000080000000 -- two different
		    masks.  The seats were right about the mechanism and it does not fire
		    here only because of the type.

		    WHAT ACTUALLY HOLDS: both operands are 32-bit, so the `&' is performed
		    at 32 bits either way -- `int & int' before, and after, `tag' converted
		    to `unsigned int' by the usual arithmetic conversions.  The RESULT'S
		    BIT PATTERN IS IDENTICAL; only its type changes, and it is consumed
		    solely as a truth test in `if (...)'.  So no guest-visible value moves,
		    and the repair is owed purely by the LANGUAGE. ***

		    *** THE LOOP THIS SITS IN IS THIS FILE'S OWN GRACEFUL-DEGRADATION
		    PRECEDENT -- the `dev >= 21' arm below has a commented-out
		    fatal()/exit(1) and substitutes `dev = 0' instead, and `lcabadval'
		    cites it as a transferable warrant.  The safe path contained the UB. ***  */
		for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)
			if (tag & (0x800U << dev))	/*  #511  */
				break;
		if (dev >= 21) {
			/*  fatal("[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\n");
			exit(1);  */
			dev = 0;
		}
	} else {
		/*  #515: a type-1 cycle carries its destination in the address.  */
		bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;	/*  #515  */
		dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;	/*  #515  */
	}

	func = (tag >> 8) & 7;
	/*  printf("bus=%i dev=%i func=%i reg=%i\n", bus,dev,func,reg);  */

	/*  #515: a type-1 cycle whose bus field is zero selects NOTHING.  Type-1 is
	    how a bridge is asked to reach a bus BEHIND it; the primary bus is reached
	    with type-0 cycles, whose IDSEL encoding is decoded above.  Forwarding this
	    case would set cur_bus = 0 and hand the access to whichever primary-bus
	    device matched -- `i82378zb' is registered at 0:7:0 by DEVINIT below -- so
	    simply decoding `bus' and stopping there would replace a host kill with a
	    NEW MISROUTING.  Refuse it instead, matching `bus_pci_data_access''s own
	    empty-slot contract (bus_pci.c, the `dev == NULL' arm: all-ones at register
	    0, zero elsewhere) so the guest sees the ordinary "nothing there" answer.
	    Writes are discarded, which leaves backend state untouched.  */
	if (type1 && bus == 0) {		/*  #515  */
		odata = reg == 0? (uint64_t) -1 : 0;
	} else {
		/*  Pass PCI accesses onto bus_pci:  */
		bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pci_data, bus, dev, func, reg);
		bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pci_data, writeflag == MEM_READ?
		    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
{
	unsigned int ofs, i;
	uint8_t byte;

	relative_addr >>= 5;

	ofs = relative_addr & 3;
	if (ofs > len) {
		fatal("[ ofs=%i len=%i in lca_isa access function. "
		    "aborting ]\n", ofs, len);
		exit(1);
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
		byte = data[ofs % len];
		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
	}

	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);

	for (i=0; i<len; i++)
		data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;

	return 1;
}


/*
 *  #518 (lcabadval): one bounded announce per (device instance, reason).  Six
 *  IOC write arms used to end the host with fatal()+exit(1) on any value outside
 *  a tiny accepted set -- and the sets were WRONG, not merely harsh: the 21066
 *  HRM (Table 6-6) lists THIRTEEN legal W_MASK encodings where the model took
 *  one, and S6.4.14 REQUIRES SROM to write ZERO to W_BASE1, which was fatal.
 *  The arms below store the ARCHITECTED FIELDS, ignore reserved bits (type X),
 *  validate W_MASK by its field rule, and decline what the model cannot honour
 *  with this announce -- fatal() prints and returns (it cannot be silenced by
 *  -q, which is why a latch and not a verbosity flag).
 */
static void lca_ioc_announce(struct lca_data *d, int bit, const char *what,
	uint64_t v)
{
	if (d->warned & (1u << bit))
		return;
	d->warned |= 1u << bit;
	fatal("[ lca_ioc: %s: 0x%" PRIx64 "  (once) ]\n", what, v);
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)
{
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	switch (relative_addr + LCA_IOC_BASE) {

	case LCA_IOC_BASE:
		/*  Ignore? Linux reads from the base at startup.  */
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_CONF:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->ioc_conf;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was `if (idata & ~1) { fatal(); exit(1); }' with the
			    store BEFORE the check (#506 had widened its diagnostic).
			    HRM S6.2.9: IOC_CONF<1:0> select the configuration cycle
			    TYPE; everything above is reserved and is IGNORED, not
			    fatal.  Bit 0 selects type-1 cycles and is what lca_pci_conf
			    reads (#515); bit 1 selects the CFG AD<1:0> encodings 10/11,
			    which are not configuration-cycle types -- stored and
			    announced once, not modelled.  (Pass 2: this comment first
			    called bit 1 the type-1 select; grok read lca_pci_conf.)  */
			d->ioc_conf = idata & 0x3ULL;
			if (idata & 0x2ULL)
				lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_CONF_CFG1, "IOC_CONF<1> set"
				    " (CFG AD<1:0> = 10/11, not a configuration-cycle type):"
				    " stored, not modelled", idata);
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_TBIA:
		/*  TLB Invalidate All.  */
		/*  TODO: For now, let's just ignore it.  */
		break;
			    the rest are type X -- IGNORED on write, never fatal.  The
			    old arm stored the whole value and then died on it.  */
			d->tlb_enable = idata & IOC_TB_ENA_TEN;
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_BASE0:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_base_0;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was a two-value whitelist + exit(1).  The HRM defines
			    this register by FIELDS -- W_BASE<31:20>, SG<32>, WEN<33> --
			    not by a list of numbers, and S6.4.14 REQUIRES SROM to write
			    zero here.  Store the fields (reserved bits dropped); the
			    window is not translated by this model, which is announced
			    once when any window is ENABLED.  */
			d->window_base_0 = idata & (IOC_W_BASE_W_BASE | IOC_W_BASE_SG | IOC_W_BASE_WEN);
			if (idata & IOC_W_BASE_WEN)
				lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WEN, "a DMA window was"
				    " enabled; window translation is not modelled", idata);
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_MASK0:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_mask_0;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was `!= 0x700000ULL' + exit(1): ONE accepted value where
			    HRM Table 6-6 lists THIRTEEN (W_MASK<31:20> = 0, 1, 3, 7, ...
			    0xfff: a contiguous low-ones field), and the old arm STORED
			    before it checked.  Validate the field, store only when it is
			    legal, decline once otherwise with the previous value kept.  */
			{
				uint64_t v = idata & 0xfff00000ULL, f = v >> 20;
				if ((f & (f + 1)) == 0)
					d->window_mask_0 = v;
				else
					lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WMASK0, "unsupported"
					    " IOC_W_MASK0 encoding declined (HRM Table 6-6);"
					    " previous value kept", idata);
			}
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_T_BASE0:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_t_base_0;
		} else {
			d->window_t_base_0 = idata;
			/*  TODO: Actually implement this.  */
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_BASE1:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_base_1;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was a ONE-value whitelist + exit(1) -- and the one value
			    was not zero, which S6.4.14 item 3 requires SROM to write.
			    Same disposition as W_BASE0 above.  */
			d->window_base_1 = idata & (IOC_W_BASE_W_BASE | IOC_W_BASE_SG | IOC_W_BASE_WEN);
			if (idata & IOC_W_BASE_WEN)
				lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WEN, "a DMA window was"
				    " enabled; window translation is not modelled", idata);
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_MASK1:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_mask_1;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was `!= 0x3ff00000ULL' + exit(1): ONE accepted value where
			    HRM Table 6-6 lists THIRTEEN (W_MASK<31:20> = 0, 1, 3, 7, ...
			    0xfff: a contiguous low-ones field), and the old arm STORED
			    before it checked.  Validate the field, store only when it is
			    legal, decline once otherwise with the previous value kept.  */
			{
				uint64_t v = idata & 0xfff00000ULL, f = v >> 20;
				if ((f & (f + 1)) == 0)
					d->window_mask_1 = v;
				else
					lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WMASK1, "unsupported"
					    " IOC_W_MASK1 encoding declined (HRM Table 6-6);"
					    " previous value kept", idata);
			}
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_T_BASE1:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_t_base_1;
		} else {
			d->window_t_base_1 = idata;
			/*  TODO: Actually implement this.  */
		}
		break;

	default:
		/*  #518: was fatal()+exit(1) for ANY offset the switch does not
		    list.  Decline the write (no state changes), answer a read with
		    zero (odata is already 0), announce each direction once.  The
		    value keeps #506's full width.  NOT all of these offsets are
		    unimplemented HOLES: STAT0 (+0x040), STAT1 (+0x060), SFT_RST
		    (+0x0c0) and PAR_DIS (+0x0e0) are DOCUMENTED registers this
		    model has never had, and S6.4.14 items 1-2 require SROM to
		    write SFT_RST and STAT0 -- those now survive as declined writes
		    instead of ending the process, and are filed (lcaiocstat0) for
		    an implementation, which this decline is not.  (grok, pass 2)  */
		{
			int bit = writeflag == MEM_WRITE ?
			    LCA_W_IOC_DEF_W : LCA_W_IOC_DEF_R;
			if (!(d->warned & (1u << bit))) {
				d->warned |= 1u << bit;
				fatal("[ lca_ioc: unimplemented %s to offset 0x%x",
				    writeflag == MEM_WRITE? "write" : "read", (int)
				    relative_addr);
				if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
					fatal(": 0x%" PRIx64 " -- declined",
					    (uint64_t)idata);	/*  #506  */
				else
					fatal(" -- answered with zero");
				fatal("  (once per direction) ]\n");
			}
		}
		break;
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}


DEVINIT(lca)
{
	char *interrupt_path;
	struct interrupt interrupt_template;
	struct lca_data *d;

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct lca_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct lca_data)));
	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct lca_data));

	/*  Register a PCI bus:  */
	d->pci_data = bus_pci_init(
	    devinit->machine,
	    "TODO: irq"		/*  pciirq: TODO  */,
	    LCA_PCI_SIO,	/*  pci device io offset  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  pci device mem offset: TODO  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI portbase: TODO  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI membase: TODO  */
	    "TODO: pci irq base",	/*  PCI irqbase: TODO  */
	    LCA_ISA_BASE,	/*  ISA portbase  */
	    LCA_ISA_MEMBASE,	/*  ISA membase  */
	    "TODO: irqbase isa");                /*  ISA irqbase: TODO  */

	/*  Add the "sio0" controller (as seen by NetBSD):  */
	bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pci_data, devinit->machine->memory,
	    0, 7, 0, "i82378zb");

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_pci_conf",
	    LCA_PCI_CONF, 0x20000000, dev_lca_pci_conf_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_isa",
	    LCA_PCI_SIO, 0x10000 << 5, dev_lca_isa_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_ioc",
	    LCA_IOC_BASE, 0x20000000, dev_lca_ioc_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(interrupt_path = (char *)
	    malloc(strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10));
	snprintf(interrupt_path, strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10,
	    "%s.lca", devinit->machine->path);

	memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
	interrupt_template.line = 0;
	interrupt_template.name = interrupt_path;
	interrupt_template.extra = d;
	interrupt_template.interrupt_assert = lca_interrupt_assert;
	interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert = lca_interrupt_deassert;
	interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);

	bus_isa_init(devinit->machine, interrupt_path,
	    BUS_ISA_IDE0 | BUS_ISA_IDE1, LCA_ISA_BASE, LCA_ISA_MEMBASE);

	return 1;
}

VERDICT: Use `ofs >= len` with member 3, and return downstream failure before packing the byte. S3�?Ts length census is incomplete in this tree; the uninitialized-byte claim is conditional.

I read the working-tree [dev_lca.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:275), its uncommitted diff, the [earlier assessment](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/codex_assess_20260908.txt), and the relevant Alpha, memory-dispatch, ISA-device and probe code. The inlined function matches. Its exit is the file�?Ts sole remaining executable `exit(1)`. The diff leaves this handler unchanged; strictly, it also adds IOC-related definitions and a helper outside `lca_ioc`.

I take the adjudication date and option taxonomy from your packet. Assignment of this exit to `lcaisalane` is corroborated by `lca_badval_probe.py:349`. This was source review plus arithmetic enumeration, without changes or emulator execution.

For Q1, distinguish the incoming sparse offset `r` from the decoded port `p = r >> 5`: **`ofs = p & 3`, hence incoming address bits 5�?"6.**

The carrier�?Ts 8201-to-6144 example concerns **MIPS** alignment exceptions. Alpha�?Ts apparent alignment gate calls the generic helper on misalignment; that helper calls `memory_rw`. It does not reject the access. Furthermore, even enforcing width alignment would leave all four `ofs` values possible, because widths through eight constrain lower address bits. See the [Alpha helper](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c:183) and [carrier example](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/BRIEF_TEMPLATE.md:75).

For the four lengths stipulated in S3, the complete table is below. `B[j]` means a `len`-byte buffer containing downstream byte `b` at index `j`, with every other byte zero. Read results assume successful downstream completion.

| `(len, ofs)` | Today | Write selects | Read produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(1,0)` | Accepted | `data[0]` | `B[0]` |
| `(1,1)` | Accepted | `data[0]` | All zeros |
| `(1,2)` | Process exit | Nothing | No completion |
| `(1,3)` | Process exit | Nothing | No completion |
| `(2,0)` | Accepted | `data[0]` | `B[0]` |
| `(2,1)` | Accepted | `data[1]` | `B[1]` |
| `(2,2)` | Accepted | `data[0]` | All zeros |
| `(2,3)` | Process exit | Nothing | No completion |
| `(4,0)` | Accepted | `data[0]` | `B[0]` |
| `(4,1)` | Accepted | `data[1]` | `B[1]` |
| `(4,2)` | Accepted | `data[2]` | `B[2]` |
| `(4,3)` | Accepted | `data[3]` | `B[3]` |
| `(8,0)` | Accepted | `data[0]` | `B[0]` |
| `(8,1)` | Accepted | `data[1]` | `B[1]` |
| `(8,2)` | Accepted | `data[2]` | `B[2]` |
| `(8,3)` | Accepted | `data[3]` | `B[3]` |

Every accepted operation attempts **one downstream byte**, regardless of container width. For unsplit integer loads, the guest receives the corresponding little-endian value; four-byte loads additionally sign-extend. Writes return downstream status.

**But those sixteen pairs are not the whole dispatcher domain.** The [memory wrapper](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:183) splits translated accesses at 1024-byte boundaries. With Alpha permitting misaligned accesses:

- Starts inside the window additionally deliver `(3,0)`, `(3,3)`, `(5,0)`, `(5,3)`, `(6,3)` and `(7,3)`.
- Accesses straddling into the window can additionally deliver `(6,0)` and `(7,0)`. For starts already inside it, the old exit prevents the corresponding short first chunks from continuing.
- These additional pairs are accepted today. `(3,3)` selects `data[0]` on writes and produces zeros on reads. Every other additional pair selects `data[ofs]` and produces `B[ofs]`.

For split accesses, these buffers are chunks of the instruction�?Ts buffer; the whole guest result depends on the other chunks and their completion. S3 is therefore usable as an explicitly restricted subdomain, not an unconditional repository fact.

Neither the modulo write nor the bounded read loop establishes an out-of-bounds access for these positive lengths. Also, **at equality the ternary never evaluates `byte`**: the equality defect and failed-byte consumption occur on different paths.

For Q2(a), **`ofs >= len` is the correct rejection predicate for this handler�?Ts existing contract that the selected lane must exist in the supplied buffer.** That is a model-support boundary, not proof of complete LCA hardware semantics.

I recommend **member 3: decline plus a latched announcement**. Before any downstream call, reject the unavailable lane, leave device state untouched, and return `0` for either direction. Define the read buffer as zero on failure. Announce once per LCA instance using a fresh bit in its existing `warned` field; bit 8 is currently available. The unconditional decline must sit **outside** the announcement�?Ts latch condition, so subsequent refusals cannot fall through. Use `%u` for `ofs` and `%zu` for `len`.

Changing only `>` to `>=` while retaining the exit expands termination to the equality cases.

For Q2(b), a failed downstream read must return **`0`**, matching the existing write arm�?Ts propagation. On this Alpha path, that is failed completion/decline, **not an architectural device fault**. The [dispatcher](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:595) delivers the relevant exceptions only for MIPS and M88K. The [Alpha load helper](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c:66) returns before destination-register writeback on failure.

Consequently, a zero-filled failure buffer does **not** mean the guest successfully loaded zero: its destination register remains unchanged. Propagating failure also cannot undo side effects of the downstream operation already attempted.

For Q2(c), prefer **restructuring so a failed result is never consumed**. Initializing `byte` alone would retain the false-success defect.

A concrete prose sketch against the whole function is:

1. Preserve the declarations and address decoding; clear the caller�?Ts buffer for reads only.
2. Apply `ofs >= len`; announce through the per-instance latch and return `0`.
3. Preserve the write arm�?Ts immediate downstream return. Its indexing may now use `data[ofs]`; retaining modulo is equivalent within the accepted domain.
4. Check the downstream read immediately. Return `0` on failure, before the packing loop.
5. Allow only successful reads into the existing loop and final `return 1`.

That accounts for the common tail: refused accesses and failed reads cannot reach it, and writes retain their separate return.

The qualification from September 8 survives inspection. Translation failures and device-dispatch reads already receive zero-filled buffers. The remaining gap is the nonexistent-memory halt path, which can return before filling `byte`. The outer dispatcher detects the running-to-stopped transition and suppresses normal Alpha writeback. Thus retain **conditional failed-byte consumption**, without claiming a demonstrated guest disclosure. With the checked-read structure, leaving the local declaration uninitialized is sufficient because successful one-byte `memory_rw` paths define it.

For Q3, these are proposed regression rows, not measured results:

| Fix obligation | Small regression that looks plausible | Row that distinguishes it |
|---|---|---|
| Equality rejection | Change `>=` back to `>` | Both equality cases: failure status and zero downstream calls, for reads and writes. Include `(3,3)` if covering actual split dispatch. |
| Nonterminating, persistent decline | Put `return 0` inside the announcement�?Ts �?onot yet warned�?? condition | Repeat a refused access: both attempts decline without forwarding; one attributable announcement. |
| Failure propagation | Change the failed-read `return 0` to `return 1` | A backend that fails while leaving the CPU running: handler returns failure; an integrated load preserves a nonzero destination sentinel. |
| No failed-byte consumption | Move the failure check below packing | A failing backend supplies a distinctive nonzero byte: failure output must remain zero. Packing that byte before returning failure fails this row. An unwritten-byte variant additionally checks definedness. |

The valid side also needs coverage: successful reads place the byte in each supported lane, and writes select distinguishable input bytes. Otherwise an overbroad refusal passes all rejection rows. A second LCA instance should announce independently.

With the chosen control-flow repair, deleting an optional `byte = 0` initializer is **equivalent**, not a mutation that breaks the fix. And a failure row that stops the CPU is inadequate for isolating lost status: the outer dispatcher already converts that transition to failure.

For Q4, I can provide the regression oracle, but cannot supply an unfixed-build guest-trigger recipe. No committed-build witness was measured here.

Two premises about the suggested devices need correction. The named 8042 ports and UART base all have `ofs == 0`, so those offsets themselves cannot demonstrate the equality defect. Moreover, the [existing IRQ probe](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py:213) accesses the downstream ISA mapping directly and bypasses `lca_isa`.

A rung-3 row would need actual guest execution through the sparse handler, an independently established nonzero downstream value, and observable completion. For the proposed repair, it should assert:

- The refused load preserves a nonzero destination sentinel and reaches the next guest marker.
- The refused operation does not affect downstream state.
- Repetition produces one site-specific announcement per instance.
- A matched supported access still returns the correctly packed downstream byte.

**Zero readback alone cannot distinguish the defect from a zero-filled fallback.** Callback mocks can verify status, byte consumption and call counts, but those rows do not become rung 3.

Two additional issues from the earlier assessment also remain: both forwarding calls lack `PHYSICAL` despite targeting registered physical ISA addresses, and the old diagnostic mismatches `size_t` with `%i`. Physical forwarding deserves separate validation; neither initialization nor the new guard repairs it.

 *
 *  Alpha load/store instructions.  (Included from cpu_alpha_instr_inc.c.)
 *
 *
 *  Load/store instructions have the following arguments:
 *  
 *  arg[0] = pointer to the register to load to or store from (uint64_t)
 *  arg[1] = pointer to the base register (uint64_t)
 *  arg[2] = offset (as an int32_t)
 *
 *  NOTE:
 *	Alpha byte and word loads (8- and 16-bit) are unsigned, while
 *	32-bit long words are sign-extended up to 64 bits during a load!
 */


#ifndef LS_IGNORE_OFFSET
static void LS_GENERIC_N(struct cpu *cpu, struct alpha_instr_call *ic)
{
#ifdef LS_B
	unsigned char data[1];
#endif
#ifdef LS_W
	unsigned char data[2];
#endif
#ifdef LS_L
	unsigned char data[4];
#endif
#ifdef LS_Q
	unsigned char data[8];
#endif
	uint64_t addr = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[1]);
	uint64_t data_x;

	addr += (int32_t)ic->arg[2];
#ifdef LS_UNALIGNED
	addr &= ~7;
#endif

#ifdef LS_LOAD
	/*  Load:  */
	if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, data, sizeof(data),
	    MEM_READ, CACHE_DATA)) {
		/*  #215: (Codex/Fable) memory_rw() returned 0 = the translator
		    already signalled a fault (cf. #194's no-translation return);
		    abandon the access instead of exit()ing the host.  */
		return;
	}

	data_x = data[0];
#ifndef LS_B
	data_x += (data[1] << 8);
#ifndef LS_W
	data_x += (data[2] << 16);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[3] << 24);
#ifdef LS_L
	data_x = (int64_t)(int32_t)data_x;
#endif
#ifndef LS_L
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[4] << 32);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[5] << 40);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[6] << 48);
	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[7] << 56);
#endif
#endif
#endif
	*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = data_x;
#else
	/*  Store:  */
	data_x = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
	data[0] = data_x;
#ifndef LS_B
	data[1] = data_x >> 8;
#ifndef LS_W
	data[2] = data_x >> 16;
	data[3] = data_x >> 24;
#ifndef LS_L
	data[4] = data_x >> 32;
	data[5] = data_x >> 40;
	data[6] = data_x >> 48;
	data[7] = data_x >> 56;
#endif
#endif
#endif

	if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, data, sizeof(data),
	    MEM_WRITE, CACHE_DATA)) {
		/*  #215: (Codex/Fable) memory_rw() returned 0 = the translator
		    already signalled a fault (cf. #194's no-translation return);
		    abandon the access instead of exit()ing the host.  */
		return;
	}

#ifdef LS_LLSC
#ifndef LS_LOAD
	*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = 1;
#endif
#endif

#endif
}
#endif

	x2 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N)) & mask2;

/*
 *  Copyright (C) 2003-2021  Anders Gavare.  All rights reserved.

/*
 *  memory_rw():
 *
 *  Read or write data from/to memory.
 *
 *	cpu		the cpu doing the read/write
 *	mem		the memory object to use
 *	vaddr		the virtual address
 *	data		a pointer to the data to be written to memory, or
 *			a placeholder for data when reading from memory
 *	len		the length of the 'data' buffer
 *	writeflag	set to MEM_READ or MEM_WRITE
 *	misc_flags	CACHE_{NONE,DATA,INSTRUCTION} | other flags
 *
 *  If the address indicates access to a memory mapped device, that device'
 *  read/write access function is called.
 *
 *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
 *
 *  Returns one of the following:
 *	MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED
 *	MEMORY_ACCESS_OK
 *
 *  (MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED is 0.)
 */

/*
 *  #430: THE PAGE SIZE, IN ONE PLACE.
 *
 *  It was a local inside the function.  The wrapper below needs the same number, and two
 *  copies of a page size that can silently diverge is the shape this tree has been bitten
 *  by before -- so it is hoisted to a macro and the local now reads it.  Note the Alpha
 *  value: it is 8 KB, not 4 KB, so anything that splits at "the page boundary" must use
 *  THIS and never a literal 4096.  A review seat caught exactly that hazard in the design
 *  brief, which had said "4 KB page" throughout.
 */
#ifdef MEM_ALPHA
#define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0x1fff
#else
#define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0xfff
#endif

/*
 *  #430: THE SPLIT GRANULE IS 1 KB, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME NUMBER AS THE MASK ABOVE.
 *
 *  MRW_OFFSET_MASK is the HOST-page granule: what memory_paddr_to_hostaddr() is asked for
 *  and what update_translation_table() caches.  The granule the SPLIT needs is a different
 *  thing -- the smallest unit at which a translator in this tree can send two adjacent
 *  virtual addresses to unrelated physical ones -- and it is SMALLER:
 *
 *      memory_arm.c:198-207   descriptor type 3 on a non-XScale core is a 1 KB page,
 *                             *return_paddr = (d2 & 0xfffffc00) | (vaddr & 0x3ff)
 *      memory_sh.c:116        SH4_PTEL_SZ_1K gives mask 0xfffffc00
 *      memory_mips_v2p.c      the VR41xx path likewise has 1 KB logic
 *
 *  A first draft of this split used the 4 KB mask and a review seat produced the
 *  counterexample: with a 1 KB mapping, a four-byte access at 0x13fe crosses a translation
 *  boundary but NOT a 4 KB one, so the loop never fired and the defect survived exactly
 *  where MEMORY_NOT_FULL_PAGE says it is most likely.  Splitting finer is always SAFE --
 *  each chunk is then inside one mapping for every translator here, and the worker's own
 *  host-page arithmetic is unchanged -- so the only cost is more worker calls on the slow
 *  path for a multi-page translated access, which is rare (large transfers are PHYSICAL
 *  and exempt).  Correctness at a known granule beats a faster one that is wrong on ARM.
 */
#define	MRW_SPLIT_MASK		0x3ff

/*  The one-page worker; its name is derived from MEMORY_RW because cpu_alpha.c includes
    this file TWICE in one translation unit (alpha_userland_memory_rw, then alpha_memory_rw
    via tmp_alpha_tail.c), so a plainly named static would be a redefinition.  */
#ifndef MRW_CAT2
#define	MRW_CAT2(a,b)		a##b
#define	MRW_CAT(a,b)		MRW_CAT2(a,b)
#endif
#define	MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE	MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _one_page)

/*
 *  The one relationship between the two that MUST hold, asserted at COMPILE TIME because it
 *  cannot be asserted at run time by the offline differential.
 *
 *  MEM_ALPHA is never compiled by that differential -- its fixture is 4 KB-specific -- so a
 *  review seat MEASURED that widening MRW_OFFSET_MASK, or hardcoding the worker's local back
 *  to 0xfff (exactly the drift this macro exists to prevent), leaves every row green.  A
 *  static assertion costs nothing and covers the Alpha build the test cannot reach.  The
 *  name is derived because this file is included twice per translation unit.
 */
typedef char MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _split_le_page)
    [(MRW_SPLIT_MASK) <= (MRW_OFFSET_MASK) ? 1 : -1];

static int MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags);


/*
 *  #430: TRANSLATE ONCE PER PAGE, NOT ONCE PER ACCESS.
 *
 *  The worker below translates the START vaddr and then copies the whole length from the
 *  host pointer for THAT page.  memory_paddr_to_hostaddr() returns a pointer into a 1 MB
 *  memblock, so the copy walks host memory that is contiguous PHYSICALLY while the guest's
 *  span is contiguous VIRTUALLY.  Those coincide only by luck, and the moment the length
 *  carries past the page boundary the guest reads -- or OVERWRITES -- whatever physical
 *  page happens to follow.  Measured offline against a deliberately non-monotonic map: a
 *  straddling read returned the physically-following page's bytes, and a straddling WRITE
 *  left the guest's intended page untouched while corrupting an unrelated one.  Measured
 *  too: when the tail page had no valid translation the old code returned
 *  MEMORY_ACCESS_OK anyway, so this was a MISSING FAULT as well as a wrong page.
 *
 *  The existing split at the end of the worker is NOT this guard and must not be mistaken
 *  for it: it fires at BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK (1 MB) granularity and its job is to stop the memcpy
 *  running off the end of a host allocation.
 *
 *  It is ONE BOUNDARY IN 1024, not 256: MRW_SPLIT_MASK is 0x3ff, so the split granule is
 *  1024 bytes and 1 MB / 1024 = 1024.  The old "256" came from dividing 1 MB by a 4 KB page
 *  -- a granule this split does not use, as the note above says: "The granule the SPLIT
 *  needs is a different one."  (And per that note the real page here is 8 KB anyway.)
 *
 *  Two properties of the memblock split, both MEASURED:
 *    - post-#430 it fires only on PHYSICAL accesses.  A TRANSLATED 4096-byte write across a
 *      1 MB boundary is chunked at the 1024-byte SPLIT granule, so it costs 4 translations
 *      and 4 host lookups and never reaches here; PHYSICAL costs 0 and 2.  A chunk of at
 *      most one granule, granule-aligned, cannot cross 1 MB.
 *    - its head can NEVER be a device, in either version: device dispatch `goto
 *      do_return_ok`s first.  Measured with a device straddling the memblock boundary --
 *      one device call, offset 0xffe, len 4, zero host lookups.
 *
 *  A LOOP, not recursion.  A recursive split costs a real frame per chunk when the compiler
 *  does NOT eliminate the tail call -- measured at -O0, 1,834,896 bytes of stack for a 16 MB
 *  access -- and a large enough access then dies on the stack rather than returning an
 *  answer.
 *
 *  CORRECTION, and it is the reason this paragraph is worded carefully: the first draft said
 *  "gcc does not turn the tail call into a loop at -O2".  MEASURED, IT DOES -- gcc 15.2.1
 *  performs the sibling-call optimisation at -O2 and -O3, leaving a 96-byte span.  So the
 *  loop is still the right implementation (it does not depend on an optimisation being
 *  applied, and -O0 builds and other compilers are real), but the justification was wrong,
 *  and the row that certified it was VACUOUS until the differential was given
 *  -fno-optimize-sibling-calls.  No caller in the tree passes an access large enough for
 *  this to bite today; it is insurance, and it costs about fifteen lines.
 *
 *  ONLY WHEN A TRANSLATION ACTUALLY HAPPENS.  Under PHYSICAL (or with no translate_v2p)
 *  paddr == vaddr, there is no per-page mapping to redo, and splitting would buy nothing
 *  while turning one large DMA into thousands of calls -- so those go straight through and
 *  the memblock split keeps doing its job unchanged.
 */
int MEMORY_RW(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
{
	if (!(misc_flags & PHYSICAL) && cpu->translate_v2p != NULL) {
		size_t granule = (size_t) MRW_SPLIT_MASK + 1;
		size_t offset = (size_t) (vaddr & MRW_SPLIT_MASK);

		/*  `len > granule - offset`, NOT `offset + len > granule`: the sum can
		    wrap for an absurd length (offset 1, len SIZE_MAX), which would skip
		    the loop and hand the whole thing to the worker.  The subtraction
		    cannot wrap, because offset < granule always.

		    STATED EXACTLY, because the first version of this comment implied more
		    than it delivers: this form does not SERVICE such a length, it declines
		    to mis-copy it.  A length near SIZE_MAX now iterates about 2^54 times
		    instead of silently under-splitting -- measured, a fuzz watchdog fired
		    at two million chunks.  No caller passes anything remotely like it (the
		    largest in the tree are PHYSICAL and exempt), so the case is recorded
		    and filed rather than handled.  */
		while (len > granule - offset) {
			size_t head = granule - offset;

			if (!MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, head,
			    writeflag, misc_flags)) {
				/*  #244 PROMISES THE WHOLE READ BUFFER, AND SPLITTING BROKE
				    THAT: the worker zeroes only the chunk IT was given, so a
				    failure before the last chunk used to leave the caller's
				    remaining bytes untouched -- uninitialised host stack, for
				    a caller that ignores the return value.  Unsplit, one call
				    covered the whole length.  Zero the rest here so the
				    guarantee is the same as it was.  */
				if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
					memset(data, 0, len);
				return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
			}

			vaddr += head;
			data += head;
			len -= head;
			offset = 0;
		}
	}

	return MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, len, writeflag, misc_flags);
}


static int MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
{
	const int offset_mask = MRW_OFFSET_MASK;

	int ok = 2;
	uint64_t paddr;
	int cache, no_exceptions, offset;
	unsigned char *memblock;
	int dyntrans_device_danger = 0;

	no_exceptions = misc_flags & NO_EXCEPTIONS;
	cache = misc_flags & CACHE_FLAGS_MASK;


	if (misc_flags & PHYSICAL || cpu->translate_v2p == NULL) {
		paddr = vaddr;
	} else {
		ok = cpu->translate_v2p(cpu, vaddr, &paddr,
		    (writeflag? FLAG_WRITEFLAG : 0) +
		    (no_exceptions? FLAG_NOEXCEPTIONS : 0)
		    zero (odata is already 0), announce each direction once.  The
		    value keeps #506's full width.  NOT all of these offsets are
		    unimplemented HOLES: STAT0 (+0x040), STAT1 (+0x060), SFT_RST
		    (+0x0c0) and PAR_DIS (+0x0e0) are DOCUMENTED registers this
		    model has never had, and S6.4.14 items 1-2 require SROM to
		    write SFT_RST and STAT0 -- those now survive as declined writes
		    instead of ending the process, and are filed (lcaiocstat0) for
		    an implementation, which this decline is not.  (grok, pass 2)  */
		{
			int bit = writeflag == MEM_WRITE ?
			    LCA_W_IOC_DEF_W : LCA_W_IOC_DEF_R;
			if (!(d->warned & (1u << bit))) {
				d->warned |= 1u << bit;
				fatal("[ lca_ioc: unimplemented %s to offset 0x%x",
				    writeflag == MEM_WRITE? "write" : "read", (int)
				    relative_addr);
				if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
					fatal(": 0x%" PRIx64 " -- declined",
					    (uint64_t)idata);	/*  #506  */
				else
					fatal(" -- answered with zero");
				fatal("  (once per direction) ]\n");
			}
		}
		break;
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}

	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	    LCA_IOC_BASE, 0x20000000, dev_lca_ioc_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	    malloc(strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10));

	interrupt_template.line = 0;
	interrupt_template.name = interrupt_path;
	interrupt_template.extra = d;

	interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert = lca_interrupt_deassert;
	interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);

	bus_isa_init(devinit->machine, interrupt_path,
	    BUS_ISA_IDE0 | BUS_ISA_IDE1, LCA_ISA_BASE, LCA_ISA_MEMBASE);

	return 1;
}

static int MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
{
	const int offset_mask = MRW_OFFSET_MASK;

	int ok = 2;
	uint64_t paddr;
	int cache, no_exceptions, offset;
	unsigned char *memblock;
	int dyntrans_device_danger = 0;

	no_exceptions = misc_flags & NO_EXCEPTIONS;
	cache = misc_flags & CACHE_FLAGS_MASK;


	if (misc_flags & PHYSICAL || cpu->translate_v2p == NULL) {
		paddr = vaddr;
	} else {
		ok = cpu->translate_v2p(cpu, vaddr, &paddr,
		    (writeflag? FLAG_WRITEFLAG : 0) +
		    (no_exceptions? FLAG_NOEXCEPTIONS : 0)
		    + (misc_flags & MEMORY_USER_ACCESS)
		    + (cache==CACHE_INSTRUCTION? FLAG_INSTR : 0));

		/*
		 *  If the translation caused an exception, or was invalid in
		 *  some way, then simply return without doing the memory
		 *  access:
		 */
		if (!ok) {
			/*  #244: A failed (or NO_EXCEPTIONS) translation leaves
			    the caller's read buffer untouched; zero it so
			    callers that ignore the return value (e.g. the DEC
			    PROM string helpers) consume deterministic 0 bytes
			    instead of uninitialised host stack. Same zero-fill
			    ethos as the failed-device-read path (#95).  */
			if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
				memset(data, 0, len);
			return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
		}

do_return_ok:
	return MEMORY_ACCESS_OK;
}

    the per-inclusion macros must not leak into the next inclusion.  MRW_CAT/MRW_CAT2 are
    inclusion-independent and are left defined behind their own guard.  */
#undef MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE
#undef MRW_OFFSET_MASK
#undef MRW_SPLIT_MASK

            byte address will get merged in the internal CPU write buffer if they are not
            separated by memory barrier instructions.

6.2.5 Address Phase

            All PCI transfers begin with the address phase. If the cycle immediately after
            the IOC detects both a bus idle condition and the assertion of the gnt_l signal,
            the IOC initiates a PCI transaction as follows:

            � Drives the PCI target address on the ad<31:0> pins

            � Drives a valid cycle code on the c_be_l<3:0> pins

            � Asserts the frame_l signal

            � Drives even parity for the address on the par pin in the next cycle

            If any PCI device detects an address parity error, it reports it on the PCI serr_l
            signal two cycles after the frame_l signal is asserted. (See Section 6.2.12 for
            more information.)

                                                                 Note

                  Because the chip does not provide an serr_l signal pin, external logic
                  must report serr_l assertions on one of the chip's irq<2:0> interrupt
                  signal pins.

            Configuration cycles use address stepping, which drives the address and
            command for two consecutive cycles. The frame_l signal is asserted during the
            second cycle.

6.2.6 Sparse Memory Space

            The sparse memory space is the region in which maskable, CPU-initiated PCI
            transactions occur. This region uses the low bits of the CPU address to indicate
            transfer size and uses the IOC_HAE register to extend the shifted address to
            cover the 32-bit PCI address space.

6.2.6.1 Generating PCI Addresses for Sparse Space
            CPU physical address bits <31:7> are used for PCI address bits <26:2>. To
            support various transfer size (byte enable) combinations in sparse space, the
            low-order bits of the CPU physical addresses that reference PCI address spaces
            are encoded as follows:

            � Physical address bits <6:3> generate the PCI byte enable c_be_l<3:0>
                  signals. Byte enables generated by combinations of physical address bits
                  <6:3>, not shown in Table 6-3, are unspecified.

                                                                                                                I/O Controller 6-7
    � Physical address bits <4:3> also specify transfer size.

    � Physical address bits <2:0> must be zero for transfers in sparse space.

    Table 6-3 shows how the low-order physical address bits are used to indicate
    transfer size.

Table 6-3 Byte Enable Encoding for CPU-Initiated Transfers in Sparse Space

 CPU CPU            BytePECnIables3          PCI       PCI      Transfer
a<6:5> a<4:3> Size                   Memory Cycles  I/O Cycles    Type
                     c_be_l<3:0>
                                         ad<1:0>      ad<1:0>

00  00 Byte         1110             00             00          Masked
                    1101
01  00 Byte         1011             00             01          Masked
                    0111
10  00 Byte                          00             10          Masked

11  00 Byte                          00             11          Masked

00  01 Word         1100             00             00          Masked
                    1001
01  01 Word         0011             00             01          Masked

10  01 Word                          00             10          Masked

00  10 Tribyte      1000             00             00          Masked
                    0001
01  10 Tribyte                       00             01          Masked

00  11 Longword     0000             00             00          Unmasked

11  11 Quadword     0000             00             00          Unmasked

3Byte enables are active low (asserted when shown as zero).

ad2 is 0 for the first longword and 1 for the second longword of the quadword. The quadword is normally
sent as a burst of two consecutive longwords, starting with the lower longword; in this case, only one address
(ad2 = 0) is generated. If a retry on the second longword is needed, the address would be regenerated with
ad2 = 1.

    During CPU-initiated transfers to sparse memory space, the IOC forces PCI
    address bits <1:0> to 00. This indicates to the target device that the transfer
    will use a linearly incrementing burst order.

    Although the PCI supports arbitrary byte enables, the IOC supports only the
    byte-enable combinations listed in Table 6-3.

    Masked Transfers: The following types of masked transfers are permitted in
    sparse space:

    � Longword read transfers--An LDL instruction can generate a masked PCI
         read transfer with a burst length of one.

    � Longword write transferss--An STL instruction must be used. The STL
         instruction generates a PCI write transfer with a burst length of one.

6-8 I/O Controller
            Unmasked Transfers: The following types of unmasked transfers are
            permitted in sparse space:

            � Longword read transfers--An LDL instruction can generate an unmasked
                  PCI read transfer with a burst length of one.

            � Quadword read transfers--An LDQ instruction can generate an unmasked
                  PCI read transfer with a burst length of two.

            � Longword write transfers--An STL instruction can generate an unmasked
                  PCI write transfer with a burst length of one.

            � Quadword write transfers--An STQ instruction can generate an unmasked
                  PCI write transfer with a burst length of two.

6.2.6.2 Address Extension for Sparse Space
            Because the CPU address is encoded to generate PCI byte enables, only 27
            physical address bits (CPU address bits a<31:5>) can be used to generate PCI
            address bits ad<26:0>, giving an effective PCI address space of 128 MB. This
            128-MB address space is subdivided into a 16-MB region and a 112-MB region.
            Generation of PCI address bits ad<31:27> depends on which of these regions
            are referenced, as shown in Table 6-4.

Table 6-4 Host Address Extension

   CPU       CPU         PCI
a<33:32>  a<31:29>  ad<31:27>

10        000       00000

10        001       IOC_HAE<31:27>

+/*
+ *  #518 (lcabadval): one bounded announce per (device instance, reason).  Six
+ *  IOC write arms used to end the host with fatal()+exit(1) on any value outside
+ *  a tiny accepted set -- and the sets were WRONG, not merely harsh: the 21066
+ *  HRM (Table 6-6) lists THIRTEEN legal W_MASK encodings where the model took
+ *  one, and S6.4.14 REQUIRES SROM to write ZERO to W_BASE1, which was fatal.
+ *  The arms below store the ARCHITECTED FIELDS, ignore reserved bits (type X),
+ *  validate W_MASK by its field rule, and decline what the model cannot honour
+ *  with this announce -- fatal() prints and returns (it cannot be silenced by
+ *  -q, which is why a latch and not a verbosity flag).
+ */
+static void lca_ioc_announce(struct lca_data *d, int bit, const char *what,
+	uint64_t v)
+{
+	if (d->warned & (1u << bit))
+		return;
+	d->warned |= 1u << bit;
+	fatal("[ lca_ioc: %s: 0x%" PRIx64 "  (once) ]\n", what, v);
+}
+
