#!/usr/bin/env python3
#
#  lcaisalane -- RUNG-3 WITNESS: the LCA sparse-I/O byte-lane handler ends the host process on an
#  ordinary byte access to a documented ISA register, and SILENTLY ANSWERS ZERO on another.
#
#  THE DEFECT, quoted from `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)' in src/devices/dev_lca.c:
#
#      relative_addr >>= 5;
#
#      ofs = relative_addr & 3;
#      if (ofs > len) {
#              fatal("[ ofs=%i len=%i in lca_isa access function. "
#                  "aborting ]\n", ofs, len);
#              exit(1);
#      }
#
#      if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
#              byte = data[ofs % len];
#              return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
#                  relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
#      }
#
#      cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
#          &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);
#
#      for (i=0; i<len; i++)
#              data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;
#
#  *** THE LANE IS NOT AN INDEPENDENT FIELD.  IT IS THE LOW TWO BITS OF THE ISA PORT NUMBER. ***
#  The shift comes FIRST, so `ofs' is bits 6:5 of the incoming relative address -- which are bits
#  1:0 of the derived port -- and the forwarded access goes to `LCA_ISA_BASE + (r >> 5)', i.e. to
#  that same port INCLUDING those two bits.  So there is no way to address ONE port through two
#  different lanes: the lane is a FUNCTION of the port.  The defect therefore partitions the whole
#  64 K ISA port space by `port & 3', and for a BYTE (len == 1) access it reads:
#
#      port & 3 == 0   ->  ofs 0.  data[0] = byte.                        CORRECT
#      port & 3 == 1   ->  ofs 1.  1 > 1 is FALSE, so ACCEPTED -- and the packing loop runs
#                          `for (i = 0; i < 1; i++) data[i] = (i == 1)? byte : 0', which is
#                          never true, so THE DEVICE BYTE IS DISCARDED AND THE GUEST IS HANDED
#                          ZERO with a success return.  A false success, not a decline.
#      port & 3 == 2   ->  ofs 2.  2 > 1  ->  fatal() + exit(1).          HOST DEATH
#      port & 3 == 3   ->  ofs 3.  3 > 1  ->  fatal() + exit(1).          HOST DEATH
#
#  For len == 2 the same arithmetic gives ofs 2 a zero answer and ofs 3 a death; for len 4 and 8
#  `ofs > len' is never true and the packing index is always in range, so those widths are clean.
#  The guard's `>' (rather than `>=') is what admits the zero-answering case: `ofs == len' passes
#  the guard and then indexes nothing.
#
#  WHAT THAT COSTS AT A REAL DEVICE, and it is why the ns16550 is the right subject.  The console
#  UART sits at ISA 0x3f8..0x3ff, so its eight registers fall into all four classes:
#
#      0x3f8 data     ofs 0   ok        0x3fc mcr       ofs 0   ok
#      0x3f9 ier      ofs 1   ZERO      0x3fd lsr       ofs 1   ZERO
#      0x3fa iir      ofs 2   DEATH     0x3fe msr       ofs 2   DEATH
#      0x3fb lctl     ofs 3   DEATH     0x3ff scratch   ofs 3   DEATH
#
#  A byte read of the LINE STATUS REGISTER -- the register a polling console driver reads before
#  every character -- returns zero, and a byte read of the MODEM STATUS or LINE CONTROL register
#  ends the emulator.  No unusual value, no driver setup, four guest instructions.
#
#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  *** WHAT THIS IS AND IS NOT.  IT IS A WITNESS, NOT A DETECTOR. ***
#
#  It establishes, on unmodified `alphabook1' with no disk image and no OS: that the sparse window
#  IS live and does reach ISA devices (`live', 0x55 from the 8042 through the same handler); that a
#  BYTE read of a device register whose port is 1 mod 4 completes successfully and hands the guest
#  zero while the device's own byte is demonstrably present (`ofs1 devsig' + `ofs1 wide'); and that
#  a byte read of a register whose port is 2 or 3 mod 4 ends the host with the arm's own diagnostic.
#
#  It is NOT a detector of any repair.  `alive' greens equally for a decline, for a latched
#  announce, for a fault and for a full implementation, and `ofs1 zero' greens for any fix that
#  hands the byte back.  A fix round owes rows that discriminate those -- at minimum, per the codex
#  design reading (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/codex_design_20260910.err): a REFUSED access must
#  leave the destination register holding its previous value (the `answer' channel this probe
#  already reads), the refusal must repeat without re-announcing, and a supported lane must still
#  deliver the correctly packed byte at every width.
#
#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  SHAPE, and every element of it is answering a specific objection:
#
#    1.  ONE PROCESS PER OUTCOME, CONTROL FIRST.  A death ends the process, so the two killing
#        lanes get their own runs -- but inside each run the DEVICE SIGNATURE and the WIDE control
#        are taken BEFORE the killing access, so between the access that lives and the access that
#        dies only the WIDTH differs: same address, same window, same process, same instant.
#    2.  ZERO READBACK ALONE PROVES NOTHING, and the design seat said so in those words.  Unmapped
#        space, a missed device and a zero-filled failure buffer all read 0 (`memory_rw.c''s #95
#        pre-zero).  So `ofs1 zero' is never asserted on its own: `ofs1 devsig' reads the SAME
#        register through the flat ISA mapping and gets a nonzero value the device alone produces,
#        and `ofs1 wide' reads it through THIS handler at len 4 and finds the byte sitting in lane
#        1 -- so the byte reaches the packing loop and the loop is what drops it.
#    3.  THE DESTINATION REGISTER IS PRE-SEEDED WITH A SENTINEL before every load.  A load that
#        FAULTS leaves the register untouched (cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c returns before
#        writeback when memory_rw fails), so `ofs1 answer' distinguishes "the model answered zero"
#        from "the access failed and the guest kept its old value".  On HEAD it is the former: a
#        FALSE SUCCESS.  This is the discriminator the design seat asked for, taken pre-fix.
#    4.  ATTRIBUTION IS BY THE ARM'S OWN TEXT, NEVER BY EXIT STATUS.  `in lca_isa access function'
#        appears exactly once in src/.  A death for any other reason -- a bad encoding, a different
#        arm -- reds the attrib row instead of passing as a reproduction.
#    5.  EVERY ACCESS IS A GUEST INSTRUCTION.  `lca_isa' is registered DM_DEFAULT, so debugger
#        `dump' would skip the handler entirely and read the pre-zero.  Every `put d' below targets
#        SCRATCH, which is RAM.
#    6.  STAY ON `step'.  Nothing here needs a host timer: the 8042 reply and the UART register
#        file are synchronous with the guest access.
#
#  THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS ISA PORT 0x300 (`ctl'), which is 0 mod 4 and has NO device behind it.
#  It survives and answers zero.  That is what attributes the two deaths to the LANE rather than to
#  the sparse window, to the byte width, to K0SEG, or to hitting empty ISA space -- and note the
#  two killing ports DO have a device behind them, so the pair brackets the question from both
#  sides.
#
#  Usage:  lca_isalane_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
#          LCAISALANE_RAW=<dir>   -- optional: dump each session's raw pty transcript there
import os, sys, pty, select, time, re

BIN   = sys.argv[1]
LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
MACH  = "alphabook1"          #  device_add(machine, "lca") is ST_ALPHABOOK1 ONLY
RAW   = os.environ.get("LCAISALANE_RAW", "")

rows = []
def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-14s %-24s %-24s %-56s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- encoder (memory format only)
ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4, T5, T6 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7
#  T6 is the highest used.  A probe reaching for an undefined register name dies with a NameError
#  rather than emitting a bad encoding, which is the safe direction (#516 found this the hard way).

def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
    assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
    return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)

def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
def LDBU(ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0a, ra, rb, disp)   # BWX byte load  -> memory_rw len 1
def LDL (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x28, ra, rb, disp)   # longword load  -> memory_rw len 4
def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)   # quadword load  -> memory_rw len 8
def STB (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0e, ra, rb, disp)   # BWX byte store -> memory_rw len 1
#  LDBU/STB are opcodes 0x0a/0x0e and are decoded with `loadstore_type = 0' in
#  cpu_alpha_instr.c, which selects the 1-byte template in cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c -- so the
#  handler under test really does see len == 1.  Not a new capability: lca_irqstub_probe.py already
#  drives STB on this machine, and there is no BWX gate in this tree.

def load_const(reg, val):
    #  32-bit only, and deliberately so: this builds SCRATCH pointers and sentinels, never a
    #  64-bit operand.  Every 64-bit address this probe drives arrives via `put d' + LDQ.
    lo = val & 0xffff
    hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
    if lo >= 0x8000:
        hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
    ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
    if lo:
        ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
    return ops

SENT = 0x0bad1dea         #  the pre-seed.  A load that FAULTS leaves this in the register.
def sent(reg): return load_const(reg, SENT)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- addresses
K0SEG        = 0xfffffc0000000000
def k0(p):   return K0SEG | p

LCA_PCI_SIO  = 0x1c0000000               # alpha_lcareg.h; DEVINIT maps 0x10000<<5 bytes here as
                                         # "lca_isa" -- THE HANDLER UNDER TEST
LCA_ISA_BASE = LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x10000000  # dev_lca.c:47.  bus_isa_init put the real devices here,
                                         # and this is where the handler FORWARDS to.
def SP(port):   return LCA_PCI_SIO  + (port << 5)   # the guest-visible sparse address
def FLAT(port): return LCA_ISA_BASE + port          # the internal flat mapping

#  ---- the ports, and why each one ----------------------------------------------------------
KBC_CMD, KBC_DAT = 0x64, 0x60      # 8042: both 0 mod 4.  0xAA is the standard self-test command
KBC_SELFTEST, KBC_REPLY = 0xAA, 0x55

regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-28-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-29-#  *** THE LANE IS NOT AN INDEPENDENT FIELD.  IT IS THE LOW TWO BITS OF THE ISA PORT NUMBER. ***
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-30-#  The shift comes FIRST, so `ofs' is bits 6:5 of the incoming relative address -- which are bits
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-31-#  1:0 of the derived port -- and the forwarded access goes to `LCA_ISA_BASE + (r >> 5)', i.e. to
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-32-#  that same port INCLUDING those two bits.  So there is no way to address ONE port through two
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-33-#  different lanes: the lane is a FUNCTION of the port.  The defect therefore partitions the whole
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:34:#  64 K ISA port space by `port & 3', and for a BYTE (len == 1) access it reads:
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-35-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-36-#      port & 3 == 0   ->  ofs 0.  data[0] = byte.                        CORRECT
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-37-#      port & 3 == 1   ->  ofs 1.  1 > 1 is FALSE, so ACCEPTED -- and the packing loop runs
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-38-#                          `for (i = 0; i < 1; i++) data[i] = (i == 1)? byte : 0', which is
--
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-40-#                          ZERO with a success return.  A false success, not a decline.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-41-#      port & 3 == 2   ->  ofs 2.  2 > 1  ->  fatal() + exit(1).          HOST DEATH
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-42-#      port & 3 == 3   ->  ofs 3.  3 > 1  ->  fatal() + exit(1).          HOST DEATH
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-43-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:44:#  For len == 2 the same arithmetic gives ofs 2 a zero answer and ofs 3 a death; for len 4 and 8
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:45:#  `ofs > len' is never true and the packing index is always in range, so those widths are clean.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:46:#  The guard's `>' (rather than `>=') is what admits the zero-answering case: `ofs == len' passes
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-47-#  the guard and then indexes nothing.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-48-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-49-#  WHAT THAT COSTS AT A REAL DEVICE, and it is why the ns16550 is the right subject.  The console
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-50-#  UART sits at ISA 0x3f8..0x3ff, so its eight registers fall into all four classes:
--
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-63-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-64-#  It establishes, on unmodified `alphabook1' with no disk image and no OS: that the sparse window
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-65-#  IS live and does reach ISA devices (`live', 0x55 from the 8042 through the same handler); that a
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-66-#  BYTE read of a device register whose port is 1 mod 4 completes successfully and hands the guest
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:67:#  zero while the device's own byte is demonstrably present (`ofs1 devsig' + `ofs1 wide'); and that
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-68-#  a byte read of a register whose port is 2 or 3 mod 4 ends the host with the arm's own diagnostic.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-69-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-70-#  It is NOT a detector of any repair.  `alive' greens equally for a decline, for a latched
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:71:#  announce, for a fault and for a full implementation, and `ofs1 zero' greens for any fix that
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-72-#  hands the byte back.  A fix round owes rows that discriminate those -- at minimum, per the codex
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-73-#  design reading (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/codex_design_20260910.err): a REFUSED access must
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-74-#  leave the destination register holding its previous value (the `answer' channel this probe
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-75-#  already reads), the refusal must repeat without re-announcing, and a supported lane must still
--
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-83-#        are taken BEFORE the killing access, so between the access that lives and the access that
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-84-#        dies only the WIDTH differs: same address, same window, same process, same instant.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-85-#    2.  ZERO READBACK ALONE PROVES NOTHING, and the design seat said so in those words.  Unmapped
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-86-#        space, a missed device and a zero-filled failure buffer all read 0 (`memory_rw.c''s #95
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:87:#        pre-zero).  So `ofs1 zero' is never asserted on its own: `ofs1 devsig' reads the SAME
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-88-#        register through the flat ISA mapping and gets a nonzero value the device alone produces,
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:89:#        and `ofs1 wide' reads it through THIS handler at len 4 and finds the byte sitting in lane
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-90-#        1 -- so the byte reaches the packing loop and the loop is what drops it.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-91-#    3.  THE DESTINATION REGISTER IS PRE-SEEDED WITH A SENTINEL before every load.  A load that
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-92-#        FAULTS leaves the register untouched (cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c returns before
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:93:#        writeback when memory_rw fails), so `ofs1 answer' distinguishes "the model answered zero"
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-94-#        from "the access failed and the guest kept its old value".  On HEAD it is the former: a
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-95-#        FALSE SUCCESS.  This is the discriminator the design seat asked for, taken pre-fix.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-96-#    4.  ATTRIBUTION IS BY THE ARM'S OWN TEXT, NEVER BY EXIT STATUS.  `in lca_isa access function'
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-97-#        appears exactly once in src/.  A death for any other reason -- a bad encoding, a different
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:98:#        arm -- reds the attrib row instead of passing as a reproduction.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-99-#    5.  EVERY ACCESS IS A GUEST INSTRUCTION.  `lca_isa' is registered DM_DEFAULT, so debugger
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-100-#        `dump' would skip the handler entirely and read the pre-zero.  Every `put d' below targets
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-101-#        SCRATCH, which is RAM.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-102-#    6.  STAY ON `step'.  Nothing here needs a host timer: the 8042 reply and the UART register
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-103-#        file are synchronous with the guest access.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-104-#
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py-105-#  THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS ISA PORT 0x300 (`ctl'), which is 0 mod 4 and has NO device behind it.
regress/lca_isalane_probe.py:106:#  It survives and answers zero.  That is what attributes the two deaths to the LANE rather than to

	}

	// debug("base = 0x%016"PRIx64"\n", base);
if (vaddr == 0xfffffffd80000000ULL) fatal("AYONA3\n");

	uint64_t addr, pte1, pte2, pte3;
	int i1, i2, i3;
	unsigned char *pt_entry_ptr;
	const int pageSizeShift = 13;
	// const int pageSize = 1 << pageSizeShift;
	const int bitsPerLevel = 10;
	const int maskPerLevel = (1 << bitsPerLevel) - 1;

	i1 = (vaddr >> (pageSizeShift + 2 * bitsPerLevel)) & maskPerLevel;
	i2 = (vaddr >> (pageSizeShift + 1 * bitsPerLevel)) & maskPerLevel;
	i3 = (vaddr >> (pageSizeShift + 0 * bitsPerLevel)) & maskPerLevel;

	// debug("vaddr=%016llx i1=0x%x i2=0x%x i3=0x%x\n", (long long)vaddr, i1, i2, i3);

	addr = base + i1 * sizeof(uint64_t);
	
	pt_entry_ptr = memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(cpu->mem, addr, 0);
	if (pt_entry_ptr == NULL)
		goto not_found;

	pte1 = *(uint64_t *)(pt_entry_ptr);
	pte1 = LE64_TO_HOST(pte1);

	// debug("pte1 = 0x%016"PRIx64"\n", pte1);
	if (!(pte1 & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)) {
		// TODO:
		// IF level1_pte<KRE> EQ 0 THEN
		//   {initiate Access Violation fault}
		// ELSE
		//   {initiate Translation Not Valid fault}

		// fatal("TODO: pte1 not valid.\n");
		goto not_found;
	}

	addr = ((pte1 >> 32) << ALPHA_PAGESHIFT) + (i2 * sizeof(uint64_t));

	pt_entry_ptr = memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(cpu->mem, addr, 0);
	if (pt_entry_ptr == NULL)
		goto not_found;

	pte2 = *(uint64_t *)(pt_entry_ptr);
	pte2 = LE64_TO_HOST(pte2);

	// debug("pte2 = 0x%016"PRIx64"\n", pte2);
	if (!(pte2 & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)) {
		// TODO:
		// IF level2_pte<KRE> EQ 0 THEN
		//   {initiate Access Violation fault}
		// ELSE
		//   {initiate Translation Not Valid fault}

		fatal("TODO: pte2 not valid.\n");
		goto not_found;
	}

	addr = ((pte2 >> 32) << ALPHA_PAGESHIFT) + (i3 * sizeof(uint64_t));

	pt_entry_ptr = memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(cpu->mem, addr, 0);
	if (pt_entry_ptr == NULL)
		goto not_found;

	pte3 = *(uint64_t *)(pt_entry_ptr);
	pte3 = LE64_TO_HOST(pte3);

	// debug("pte3 = 0x%016"PRIx64"\n", pte3);

	if (!(pte3 & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)) {
		fatal("TODO: pte3 not valid.\n");
		goto not_found;
	}

	/*  #508: was just `PFN << pageSizeShift' -- a PAGE BASE.  memory_rw then takes
	    `offset = paddr & offset_mask', which was therefore always zero, so every
	    walked access FILLED THE TRANSLATION FROM the page's first bytes.  ("every
	    walked access resolved to" until 2026-09-07: a pass-2 seat noted that the
	    Alpha FAST PATH recomputes `addr & 8191' itself, so a later cache HIT reads
	    the right bytes.  The corruption is the SLOW-PATH FILL, which is also why a
	    watchpoint -- which keeps a page off the fast store table -- makes it
	    reproducible.)  Measured: a cold guest ldq
	    of vaddr+8 returned the vaddr+0 value.  A debugger watchpoint on a walked page
	    landed at the base for the same reason.
	
	    The mask is derived from pageSizeShift rather than written as 0x1fff: this
	    function already has TWO names for 13 (ALPHA_PAGESHIFT and this local), and a
	    literal beside the shift is how the two drift.  `|' rather than `+' because
	    that is what every other CPU's walk in this tree writes; here they are equal,
	    the shifted PFN having bits 12:0 clear.  The TODO below STAYS: `flags' is
	    still ignored, the protection bits untested, and `return 2' still reports
	    every walked page writable.  */
	(*return_paddr) = (ALPHA_PTE_TO_PFN(pte3) << pageSizeShift)
	    | (vaddr & ((1ULL << pageSizeShift) - 1));

	// TODO.
	return 2;

	    raises the fault (keeps the emulator alive and debuggable).  */
	fatal("[ alpha_translate_v2p: 0x%016" PRIx64" wasn't found ]\n", vaddr);

The four \"accepts exactly one value\" registers need a firmware revision that writes something else; these\ntwo need only that an interrupt is asserted.\n\n*A row named after one class hides the members of the others. IT CORRECTED THREE OF THE FIVE PREMISES I HAD\n=== JUST RE-DERIVED, and one of the corrections changes what this round may even choose from.\n\n**SEVERITY ORDER CONFIRMED: the two INTERRUPT STUBS first** -- but \"provisionally; firmware execution order is\nunproved\", which is the honest qualifier my own framing lacked.\n\n*** A SEVENTH OPTION-SET MEMBER: IMPLEMENT. ***  The carrier's set is DELETE / DECLINE / DECLINE+LATCHED\nANNOUNCE / ACCEPT / FAULT / BACKED ACCEPT -- all six presume the access is a REFUSAL DECISION. So: double quotes lose to backticks, single quotes lose to\napostrophes, and English prose about shell quoting reliably contains both.\n\n**The rule is not a better quoting style.  It is: PROSE DESTINED FOR A RECORD GOES INTO A SCRIPT\nFILE, never through `-c`.**  This paragraph was written that way and landed intact on the first\nattempt.\n\n*** PASS-1 DESIGN PANEL 2026-09-07 (preserved: regress/artifacts/lcabadval/panel_p1_20260907/ -- _scratchpad is never committed). That is checkable and should be checked before either\nis adopted.\n\n**THE DISSENT, recorded as a dissent and not as noise:** deepseek alone says premise 6 is unsound\nfor all six -- *\"the guard's accepted-value set is the model's CONTRACT.  Storing a rejected value\nand returning it on readback creates a new false belief that the value is supported, even if\naccepted values are also unimplemented.  The existing lie for accepted values does not license\nexpanding the set of lies.\"*  It also warns the reasoning generalises: it would justify removing any\nstore-before-guard exit anywhere.  **That generalisation objection is the strongest single argument\nagainst A and is not answered by the five-versus-one split**, because it attacks the warrant rather\nthan its scope.\n\n**A BRIEF DEFECT OF MINE, named by glm and correct:** premise 2's field counts were *asserted, not\ninlined* -- I ran the greps and wrote the conclusion instead of the OUTPUT.  This project's own rule\nis that a brief inlines the DERIVER'S OUTPUT rather than a literal, precisely so a packet-fed seat\ncan check it.  glm could not settle five of the six arms from the packet for exactly that reason and\nsaid so honestly rather than ratifying.\n\n**NOT ADOPTED, PENDING codex AND THE ROSTER.**  The next brief is per-arm by construction, carries\nthe deriver output rather than its summary, and puts minimax's silently-broken-consumer prediction\nas a question a measurement can answer.\n\n*** THE grok BODY, READ IN FULL 2026-09-07 -- AND NONE OF THE FOLLOWING WAS IN ITS VERDICT LINE.\nThis is the read-the-body rule paying off on the same day a row was filed about not reading. ***\n\n**1. IT ANSWERS deepseek's DISSENT, which the five/one split could not.**  deepseek's objection was\nthat premise 6 generalises -- it would license removing any store-before-guard exit anywhere.  grok\nscopes it precisely: *\"premise 6 is not the reasoning that lets every store-before-guard site keep\nits store.  It is the reasoning that lets a site keep its store WHEN NO ACCEPTED VALUE OF THAT FIELD\nIS CONSUMED.  That is #447's own per-site rule, not an evasion of it.\"*  The warrant is a per-site\ntest with a checkable predicate, not a blanket.\n\n**2. PREMISE 5 SCORED: \"The comments exist.  The transfer does not.\"**  RCR1 refuses CONSUMED\nFEATURE BITS; these guards whitelist UNIMPLEMENTED CONSTANTS.  That is the distinction the whole\nfork turned on, and it is sharper than my premise 6 offered.\n\n**3. *** OPTION A OPENS A NEW KILL PATH, AND THIS IS THE ROUND'S SAFETY FINDING. ***  Under A, a CONF\nwrite with extra bits that ALSO sets bit 0 reaches `lca_pci_conf`'s `bus != 0` exit(1) -- *\"newly\nreachable ... today that write dies in the CONF guard first.\"*  **A is not purely a reduction; it\nconverts one kill into a different reachable one.**  Any round shipping A must say so, and should\nconsider whether `lca_pci_conf` is in scope with it.\n\n**4. THE FILE'S OWN IDIOM IS A, AND THE GUARDS ARE THE OUTLIERS.**  In the SAME switch:\n`LCA_IOC_BASE` (HAE) and `TBIA` already silently ignore; `W_T_BASE0/1` already accept-and-store; and\nthe `dev >= 21` loop already deletes a kill and substitutes a default.  *\"The value guards are the\noutliers in THIS switch, not the house style.\"*  That is a stronger warrant than anything my brief\noffered, and it is one grep away.\n\n**5. THE ANNOUNCE TEXT WOULD BECOME A WRONG RECORD THE MOMENT A LANDS.**  The current string says\n*\"the only implemented value\"* about a value that **is not implemented**.  The honest once-message is\n*\"window translation / extra CONF bits not implemented\"*, not *\"illegal encoding\"*.  Rewrite it or\ndelete it with the exit -- do not carry it forward.\n\n**6. OPTION-SET CORRECTIONS.**  `#443`'s restore is *\"B wearing extra lines\"* -- observationally B\nfor readback, since nothing re-enters between the store and the `break`; not a distinct member.\n**STAT0 is the architecturally correct error path for LCA** (`IOC_STAT0_ERR` is R/W1C) and is\n**BLOCKED**: STAT0/STAT1 have no case and hit `default:` exit(1) -- *\"You cannot report an error\nthrough a register that currently kills the host.\"*  That member owes a STAT0 implementation first,\nwhich is a different round.  **FAULT confirmed unavailable**: `memory_rw.c` raises on `res <= 0` only\nunder MEM_MIPS and MEM_M88K; Alpha compiles with MEM_ALPHA and has no arm, so `return 0` fails the\naccess with no guest exception.\n\n**7. MASKING CONF IS UNLICENSED, WITH A REASON I DID NOT HAVE: THERE IS NO DATASHEET.**  No 21066\ndocument is in the packet or in the tracked `_scratchpad/*.pdf` list, so RAZ/WI versus store-full is\n**not settleable from this tree**.  Premise 3 stands and masking would be an addition without a\nwarrant.\n\n*** 8. THE WITNESS IS NOT PENDING -- THE RECIPE EXISTS.  This moves the round off documented-only. ***\n* **Machine: `alphabook1` ONLY.**  `machine_alpha.c`'s `device_add(machine, \"lca\")` is under\n  `ST_ALPHABOOK1` and no other subtype.\n* **Addresses** (K0SEG | physical, because `alpha_translate_v2p` identity-maps K0SEG in kernel mode):\n  CONF `0xfffffc0180000020`, TB_ENA `...00a0`, BASE0 `...0100`, BASE1 `...0120`, MASK0 `...0140`,\n  MASK1 `...0160`.\n* **`regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py` ALREADY issues a guest `stq`/`ldq` through this exact decode at\n  CONF on unmodified `alphabook1`**; the other five are further cases of the same DEVICE_ACCESS on\n  the same mapping.\n* **Three traps that file already paid for, so a new probe need not rediscover them:** debugger\n  `dump` skips `DM_DEFAULT` handlers; a debugger `put d` of a killing value IS a host kill but is\n  NOT a guest instruction (so it does not witness); and HAE at window offset 0 silently succeeds, so\n  **a miss control must not use `LCA_IOC_BASE+0`**.\n\n**9. THE KILL CENSUS FOR THE WHOLE FILE:** eleven live `exit(1)` today; **A removes six; five\nremain** -- the `default:` arm (both reads AND writes, covering STAT0/STAT1 and the rest of the\n512 MB IOC window), `lca_pci_conf`'s `bus != 0`, `lca_isa`'s `ofs > len` (filed as [[lcaisalane]]),\nand the bare `lca_interrupt_assert`/`_deassert` TODO pair (member 7, not this round).  Residuals not\nof this class: HAE writes discarded with no storage; TBIA ignored; `default:` still narrows\n`relative_addr` through `(int)` -- **`#506` fixed the `idata` half only**; and [[lcashiftub]].\n\n**10. A CENSUS ERROR IN THIS ROW'S OWN HISTORY:** the guards are TWO predicate shapes, not one --\nbit-masks (CONF, TB_ENA) and equality whitelists (the four windows) -- **and `W_BASE0` accepts TWO\nvalues**, so the row's original \"four registers accept exactly one value each\" framing was false of\n`BASE0` before the title was even corrected to six.  One DESIGN still covers all six (delete the\nexit, keep the store, optional latch); one design does NOT if you pick B, because *\"B is a coherent\nanswer only for a consumed field, which five of these are not.\"*\n\n*** THE codex SEAT LANDED LAST AND OVERTURNED THE PANEL ON A FACT CHECKABLE IN ONE GREP.  MY\nPREMISE 6 IS DEAD, AND SO IS THE FORMULATION I HAD CREDITED grok WITH. ***\n\n**codex's verdict:** *\"Your argument for A fails as written: RCR1 also has no functional consumer.\nI recommend B for pass 1 as a conservative policy choice; hardware-correct write/readback behavior\nremains unproved.\"*\n\n**VERIFIED HERE, not taken on trust** -- `grep -rn rtc_rcr1 src/` returns exactly FOUR occurrences:\n\n    dev_sh4.c:388    uint8_t   rtc_rcr1;              declaration\n    dev_sh4.c:2776   odata = d->rtc_rcr1;             READBACK\n    dev_sh4.c:2781   ...landed in rtc_rcr1 and...     INSIDE THE COMMENT\n    dev_sh4.c:2805   d->rtc_rcr1 = idata;             STORE\n\n**Zero functional consumers.**  And the comparison that settles it: `ioc_conf` also has four\noccurrences, but its fourth is `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` -- a real consumer.  **So RCR1 is consumed\nLESS than LCA's CONF, not more.**\n\n*** WHAT THIS DESTROYS. ***  My premise 6 claimed LCA differs from RCR1 because *\"RCR1's stored value\nhas a meaning the model otherwise honours\"*.  It has no such meaning: the model stores it and reads\nit back, exactly like the five LCA window fields.  **grok's formulation, which I recorded above as\n\"the keeper\" -- *\"RCR1 refuses CONSUMED FEATURE BITS; these guards whitelist UNIMPLEMENTED\nCONSTANTS\"* -- is FALSE IN ITS FIRST HALF.**  So is my own gloss that `#447`'s edit \"does not\ntransfer\".\n\n**IT TRANSFERS EXACTLY.**  `#447` faced a store-before-guard field with NO functional consumer,\nand chose to MOVE THE STORE BELOW THE GUARD.  That is the LCA shape, arm for arm.  The tree's own\nprecedent is therefore evidence FOR B, which is what codex, deepseek and (for CONF) glm all said.\n\n*** AND THE SURVIVING ARGUMENT IS THE ONE deepseek RAISED FIRST AND codex SHARPENED: THE GUEST IS\nITSELF A CONSUMER OF READBACK. ***  codex: *\"Previously, a rejected write ended execution before\nsubsequent guest code could observe that value.  A creates that observation and allows the guest to\nmake further decisions from it.  Existing incomplete behavior for accepted settings does not\nestablish that these newly admitted executions are equally harmless.\"*  That argument is untouched by\nthe consumer census, because it does not depend on a MODEL consumer at all -- and it is precisely\nwhat `#447`'s comment means by *\"the guest writes CIE|AIE, reads CIE|AIE back, and believes the RTC\nwill interrupt it.\"*\n\n**TALLY, and the argument decides it rather than the count:** codex -> B; deepseek -> B; glm -> B for\nCONF and not settleable for the five; kimi substitute -> split per arm; grok -> A; minimax -> A.  But\ngrok's and minimax's A both rest on the consumed/unconsumed distinction, **and that distinction is\nnow measured false.**  Count the arguments, not the seats: the load-bearing premise under the A\nposition has been refuted by a grep.\n\n**THIS IS THE THIRD REVERSAL IN THIS ROUND'S DESIGN AND IT COST NOTHING, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE\nHOLD DISCIPLINE.**  No code is committed; the round has produced adjudication text and zero product\nchanges.  The earlier records above -- premise 6 \"sound for five arms\", grok's formulation as \"the\nkeeper\" -- are left standing WITH this correction beneath them rather than silently edited, because\na future reader needs to see that the panel's majority reasoning was overturned and by what.\n\n*** WHAT IS NOW OWED BEFORE ANY FIX: the ARM-BY-ARM design, with the RCR1 precedent read as\nSUPPORTING B, and codex's readback-consumer argument answered rather than assumed away.  The\nquestion that remains genuinely open is minimax's measurable one -- under B a rejected CONF write\nretains the previous value, and codex adds that W_MASK0/W_BASE1/W_MASK1 RESET TO ZERO, which is NOT\nin their accepted set, so \"retain the last accepted value\" is not even well-defined at reset.  That\nis a concrete defect in B as stated and it needs an answer (retain? force the reset value? decline\nonly the guarded bits?) before B can ship. ***\n\n*** AND THE SHARPEST LESSON IS NOT THE REVERSAL, IT IS HOW MY GLOSS BEAT MY OWN PRIMARY SOURCE. ***\n\nThe brief inlined #447s RCR1 comment VERBATIM.  That comment argues purely from READBACK:\n\n    the guest writes CIE|AIE, reads CIE|AIE back, and believes the RTC will\n    interrupt it.  It never will.\n\n**It never claims a model consumer.**  The consumed/unconsumed framing is MINE -- premise 6 glossed\nit as RCR1s stored value having a meaning the model otherwise honours -- and grok adopted my\ngloss rather than the quoted text sitting directly above it, producing B copies RCR1s edit\nwithout RCR1s reason.  codex went to the source, grepped the field, and found the gloss false.\n\n> **INLINING THE PRIMARY SOURCE DOES NOT PROTECT A BRIEF FROM A WRONG SUMMARY PLACED BESIDE IT.**\n> A seat reads the summary as the QUESTION and the quote as SUPPORTING MATERIAL, so a false gloss\n> steers the answer even when the refuting text is on the same page.  This projects premise-audit\n> rule says to state load-bearing premises AS QUESTIONS; the refinement is that a premise which\n> RESTATES an inlined quotation is the most dangerous kind, because it looks already verified.\n\n*The cheap defence is the one codex used and I did not: when a premise characterises what some\nother code does, GREP THAT CODE.  `grep -rn rtc_rcr1 src/` is four lines of output and it was\navailable to me before the brief went out.*\n\n*** codex, `#511` PASS 2, CHALLENGES THE \"GRACEFUL DEGRADATION PRECEDENT\" THAT BOTH LCA ROWS LEAN\nON. ***\n\n*\"`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`'s fallback selects device zero and forwards the access through\n`bus_pci_setaddr`.  **That is an ALIASING DECISION, not by itself proof of harmless degradation.**\nThe comment's 'transferable warrant' needs its own justification; the shift repair does not establish\nit.\"*\n\nThe `dev >= 21` arm -- commented-out `fatal()`/`exit(1)` with `dev = 0;` substituted -- has been\ncited repeatedly here, including by [[lcabadval]], as the file's own in-tree warrant for deleting a\nhost kill.  **codex's point is that substituting device ZERO is not a decline: it silently redirects\nthe access to a DIFFERENT DEVICE.**  Whether that is harmless is a separate question nobody has\nasked, and \"someone already did it in this file\" is not the same as \"it is right\".\n\n> **A TRANSFERRED WARRANT NEEDS ITS OWN JUSTIFICATION, NOT JUST A PRECEDENT.**  This is the\n> option-set rule's own caution turned around: grepping the fork for an existing idiom finds what the\n> tree DOES, which is evidence about practice and not about correctness.\n\n*Not resolved here.  Recorded on both rows because both cite it, and any round that leans on it owes\nthe justification codex is asking for.*\n\n*** THE THIRD OPTION-SET MEMBER IS NAMED, AND THE TWO-OPTION FRAMING WAS REALLY A ONE-OPTION\nFRAMING.  codex, 2026-09-08; the two load-bearing claims VERIFIED here before recording. ***\n\n**VERDICT: C -- decline the out-of-set write, PRESERVE the previous register value, announce once.**\n\n### 1.  A AND B OVERLAP.  There was never a choice between them.\n\n*\"Your reconstructed A and B overlap: both store the written value and continue.  With the existing\nreads retained, B describes SHADOW STORAGE WITH READBACK, not a write-only register.  It supplies no\ndistinct state-transition policy.\"*  **So the panel that argued A-versus-B was arguing about one\noption under two names** -- the option-set-completeness failure in its purest form, and this row had\nit for two rounds.\n\n### 2.  *** WHAT `#447` ACTUALLY DOES IS DECLINE-AND-PRESERVE, AND I HAD IT BACKWARDS. ***\n\nThe precedent was cited as \"a register with no consumer, where the write is accepted\".  The\nimplementation says otherwise -- **the store is in the ELSE branch**:\n\n    if (idata & (SH_RCR1_CIE | SH_RCR1_AIE)) {\n            /* bounded diagnostic */\n    } else\n            d->rtc_rcr1 = idata;\n\n*\"Unsupported writes preserve the previous value.  They are NOT accepted into backing storage.\"*  And\nthe reason recorded in that comment is a READBACK concern -- echoing interrupt-enable flags would\nsuggest functionality the model does not provide -- so **absence of a consumer neither disqualifies\ndecline-and-preserve nor argues for accepting everything**: readback is guest-observable on its own.\n\n### 3.  *** `ioc_conf` HAS A FUNCTIONAL CONSUMER.  VERIFIED. ***\n\n`dev_lca.c:121` is `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;`, feeding the PCI decode.  **The row's \"no functional\nconsumer\" premise is false for CONF**, whatever it is for the other five.\n\n> **AND IT TIES TWO HIGH ROWS TOGETHER.**  `lcaconfbus1kill` is precisely about that consumer: a\n> write of `0x1` makes `bus == 1` and arms the `bus != 0` host kill.  `0x1` is INSIDE this arm's\n> accepted set `{0, 1}`, so **C does not protect against it and must not be described as if it\n> does.**  The two rows are separate defects on one register and both need fixing.\n\n### 4.  THE RESET QUESTION IS DISSOLVED, NOT ANSWERED -- and the reason improves the fix.\n\n*\"The predicates govern WRITES, not all possible stored states.\"*  Initialization assigns zero with\nno guard; reads return the stored value with no guard; **and the write assigns BEFORE the guard is\nevaluated.**  So the model can return zero at reset while refusing a later write of zero:\n*\"that asymmetry is established; it is not a logical contradiction.\"*\n\n    register  writes the guard accepts        reset 0 passes?\n    CONF      {0, 1}                          yes\n    TB_ENA    {0, 0x80}                       yes\n    W_BASE0   {0, 0x300800000}                yes\n    W_MASK0   {0x700000}                      NO\n    W_BASE1   {0x240000000}                   NO\n    W_MASK1   {0x3ff00000}                    NO\n\n*** MY OWN ADDITION, from verifying the store-order claim at `:233` vs the guard at `:236`: BECAUSE\nTHE STORE HAPPENS FIRST, SIMPLY DELETING THE `exit(1)` LEAVES THE BAD VALUE STORED. ***  That is not\na weaker version of C -- it is a DIFFERENT OBSERVABLE STATE, readable by the guest through the\nexisting read arm.  **C requires moving each store behind its guard, exactly as RCR1 does.**\n\n### 5.  THE SIBLING EVIDENCE SUPPORTS BOTH POLICIES AND SELECTS NEITHER\n\nInside this same switch:\n\n    LCA_IOC_BASE, LCA_IOC_TBIA      discard writes; reads return 0; success\n    LCA_IOC_W_T_BASE0/1             store writes and return them on reads\n    default:                        diagnose and TERMINATE, no backing storage\n\n*\"Thus the sibling evidence supports BOTH discarding writes and retaining shadow state.  It does not\nselect a universal policy.\"*  **A useful negative result: the grep-the-fork rule found the member but\ncannot settle the policy here**, because the file is internally inconsistent.\n\n*`default:` is explicitly OUT of this round -- it has no previous value to preserve, so C is not even\nexpressible there.*\n\n### 6.  THE CENSUS SURVIVES ITS AUDIT\n\nEleven live explicit `exit(1)`: the six value arms at **248, 268, 283, 298, 322, 337**, plus\n**73, 85, 167, 196, 357**.  A bare `exit(` grep returns thirteen; two are comments (the explanatory\nmention and the disabled call whose live fallback is `dev = 0`).  *This is the row's third census and\nthe first to survive.\n\n*** CORRECTION, WITHIN THE HOUR AND BEFORE THIS PROPAGATED: \"THE THIRD OPTION-SET MEMBER\" IS WRONG.\nTHERE IS NO THIRD MEMBER.  THE OPTION SET WAS ALREADY RIGHT AND **MY BRIEF CORRUPTED IT**. ***\n\n`tools/pipeline/fable_queue.md` §7i states the fork exactly as the earlier panel left it:\n\n    A  delete fatal()+exit(1) from all six arms, KEEP the store that already ran\n    B  DECLINE -- move the store BELOW the guard, as #447 did for SH4's RCR1\n\n**The queue's B IS codex's C.**  Decline-and-preserve was already ON the table, correctly described,\nwith the `#447` precedent already attached to it.  My `DISPOSITION2_brief.md` reconstructed B as\n*\"treat the register as write-only backing state\"* -- which is NOT what B says -- and that\nreconstruction is what made A and B overlap.\n\n> **SO THE OPTION-SET FAILURE WAS IN THE BRIEF, NOT IN THE ROW.**  The row had two genuinely\n> distinct members; the brief collapsed them into one and then asked the panel for a third.  *A\n> brief that mis-states the options gets a panel arguing about the wrong set* -- which is the\n> option-set rule's own warning, arriving from the direction it does not usually name: not a MISSING\n> member, an ALTERED one.\n\n**codex flagged this itself and I read past it:** *\"If the earlier panel called decline-and-preserve\n'B,' its letter assignment differs from your reconstruction. in this row's own queue entry.**\n\n*** WHAT SURVIVES THE CORRECTION, and it is nearly all of it: ***\n\n* **The DISPOSITION is unchanged and now has two independent derivations** -- the earlier dispositions from the standing\noption set.  **FAULT looks available and is not**, which is the option-set-completeness problem\ninverted -- not a missing member, a PHANTOM one.\n\n    memory_rw.c        the device-failure dispatcher has MIPS and M88K exception arms and\n                       NO ALPHA ARM                          (found via dev_lca.c, `lcaisalane`)\n    memory_alpha.c     no Recorded here so the adjudication prices it rather than assuming it.\n\n*** THE NOTE ABOVE UNDERSTATED THE SCOPE, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART: FAULT IS NOT\n\"UNAVAILABLE ON ALPHA\".  IT IS AVAILABLE ON **MIPS AND M88K ONLY**, FOR EVERY ARCHITECTURE IN THIS\nTREE.  Derived 2026-09-08 by opening the dispatcher. ***\n\n`src/cpus/memory_rw.c`, the device-failure path -- the whole of it:\n\n    if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {\n            debug(\"[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx failed ]\");\n    #ifdef MEM_MIPS\n            mips_cpu_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION? EXCEPTION_IBE : EXCEPTION_DBE, ...);\n    #endif\n    #ifdef MEM_M88K\n            m88k_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION ? M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS\n                                                           : M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS, 0);\n    #endif\n            return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;\n    }\n\n**TWO `#ifdef` ARMS.  There is no ARM arm, no Alpha arm, no PPC arm and no SH arm** -- every other\narchitecture reaches `return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED` with no exception delivered at all.\n\n> **SO OPTION-SET MEMBER 5 IS A PHANTOM ON EVERY ARCHITECTURE BUT TWO**, and a round on an ARM, SH,\n> PPC or Alpha device that reaches for FAULT is reaching for a round that does not exist yet.\n\n*Found because I wrote the OPPOSITE into a brief.  Assessing `fbfiqsilent` -- an ARM footbridge row\n-- I told the seat that \"member 5 FAULT may genuinely be available here, `memory_rw`'s\ndevice-failure path has an ARM arm.\"  It replied that the claimed arm **is absent from this tree**,\nand opening the file confirms it.  **I was one brief away from spreading a false availability claim\nacross the ARM queue**, having just corrected three rows for the Alpha version of it.*\n\n*** THE 21066 MANUAL IS LOCAL NOW, AND IT PUTS A NUMBER ON THIS ROW FOR THE FIRST TIME -- BUT THE\nNUMBER IS MY DERIVATION AND IS OUT WITH A PANEL, NOT ESTABLISHED. ***\n\n**QUOTED, `_scratchpad/21066_66a_hrm.txt:9572-9578`, read locally:**\n\n> *\"The window mask register IOC_W_MASK provides a mask corresponding to bits <31:20> of an\n> incoming PCI address.  The size of each window is programmed to be from 1 MB to 4 GB ...  Table\n> 6-6 shows the supported values of IOC_W_MASK register bits <31:20>; **other values are\n> unsupported**.\"*\n\n**Table 6-6 (`:9581-9598`) has THIRTEEN rows** -- a doubling run from 1 MB (`0x000`) to 4 GB\n(`0xfff`), each a contiguous run of low ones.\n\n**MY DERIVATION, flagged as mine:** the register value is the field shifted left 20, so the fork's\ntwo accepted constants are\n\n    LCA_IOC_W_MASK0   accepts ONLY 0x700000     ->  field 0x007  ->    8 MB   legal, 1 of 13\n    LCA_IOC_W_MASK1   accepts ONLY 0x3ff00000   ->  field 0x3ff  ->    1 GB   legal, 1 of 13\n\nand the legal set is exactly `(f & (f + 1)) == 0`, verified over all thirteen.\n\n> **IF THAT IS RIGHT, the model terminates the host on TWELVE DOCUMENTED-LEGAL VALUES PER MASK\n> REGISTER -- 24 across the two -- and a guest programming any window size other than 8 MB / 1 GB\n> is doing something the hardware manual explicitly supports.**  That is a much sharper severity\n> statement than *\"rejects values outside a small accepted set\"*, because it names the denominator.\n\n*** IT IS NOT RECORDED AS ESTABLISHED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. ***  The load-bearing step is my own\narithmetic, done in one sitting, on a table extracted by `pdftotext` -- and this project's rule is\nthat an extracted table can stagger names against values.  **Table 6-6 LOOKS self-calibrating (a\ndoubling bit-run beside a doubling size run, thirteen against thirteen), but the calibration rule\nsays one point is only valid for a UNIFORM stagger and you cannot tell which kind you have from one\npoint.**  So it went out to a panel with *\"check the premises, not just the derivations\"* and with\nthe derivation shown step by step:\n\n    regress/artifacts/lcabadval/MANUAL_brief.md   file-reading seats: codex, grok -- also asked to\n                                                  read S6.4 for the OTHER FOUR arms and to give a\n                                                  count WITH ITS DENOMINATOR per register\n    regress/artifacts/lcabadval/PACKET_brief.md   packet-fed seats: glm, deepseek, minimax, kimi --\n                                                  Table 6-6 inlined verbatim so they can check the\n                                                  arithmetic without file access\n\n**WHAT IS STILL OPEN, and the brief asks for each explicitly:** the manual enumerates legal values\nfor W_MASK and (as far as I read) NOT for W_BASE, so the two registers may need different\ndispositions; `0x300800000` and `0x240000000` are 34-bit values and I have not established how wide\nthese registers are or what bits above 31 mean; and the more dangerous error direction is a real\nquestion -- terminating on a legal value, versus accepting a value the model's own address decode\nthen does not honour.\n\n*** THE PANEL ANSWERED: SIX SEATS, MY COUNT CONFIRMED -- AND THE ONE SEAT THAT DISSENTED MADE THE\nDEFECT BIGGER, NOT SMALLER. ***\n\n    codex     CONFIRMS 12/13 per register, 24/26 My quote enumerates bits\n<31:20> and says nothing about <19:0>.  codex read the preface: **RES fields are type `X` --\nIGNORED ON WRITES, unpredictable on reads, NOT must-be-zero** (`21066_66a_hrm.txt:955`, `:985`).\n\n> **SO THE GUARD IS WORSE THAN \"ACCEPTS ONE OF THIRTEEN\".  IT TESTS THE WHOLE 64-BIT OPERAND\n> AGAINST A SINGLE LITERAL -- `if (idata != 0x700000ULL)` -- WHILE THE HARDWARE DEFINES A 12-BIT\n> FIELD AND IGNORES THE REST.**  A guest writing `0x7000ff`, functionally identical on real\n> hardware, terminates the emulator.  *deepseek said so in as many words: \"if lower bits are\n> ignored, the number of legal register values is much larger than 13 per mask, and the code is\n> even more restrictive.\"*\n\n**AND IT SUPPLIED THE TRAP A NAIVE FIX WOULD HIT:** the invariant must be applied to the SHIFTED\nFIELD.  `(idata & (idata + 1)) == 0` on the full value **REJECTS THE LEGAL `0x700000`**, because\n`0x700000 & 0x700001 == 0x700000`.  The guard is `f = idata >> 20; (f & (f + 1)) == 0`.\n\n*** FOUR MORE CORRECTIONS FROM codex, EACH NARROWING OR SHARPENING A CLAIM OF MINE. ***\n\n* **MY OWN TABLE DROPPED A ROW.**  `MANUAL_brief.md` reformatted Table 6-6 into three columns and\n  **lost `0000 0001 1111` = 32 MB** -- twelve rows under a sentence saying thirteen.  The PACKET\n  brief has all thirteen.  *A transcription error in the brief that asks others to check my\n  transcription.*\n* **ALIGNMENT AND NON-OVERLAP, which my quote stopped one paragraph short of** (`:9618`, S6.4.9):\n  bases must align naturally to window size and windows must not overlap.  **With the two accepted\n  bases held FIXED, window 0 admits only the four sizes through 8 MB and window 1 the eleven\n  through 1 GB** -- so in that configuration the guards reject 3/4 and 10/11.  All thirteen sizes\n  are supported; changing size can require changing the surrounding configuration.\n* **`IOC_CONF` IS A TWO-BIT FIELD, values 0..3, so `idata & ~1` rejects 2 of 4** defined settings --\n  I had quoted the guard without checking the field width.\n* **`IOC_TB_ENA` IS NOT A FUNCTIONAL DEFECT AT ALL: TEN has exactly two states and BOTH are\n  accepted -- 0/2.**  Its only defect is the ignored-bit one.  *Recorded because a round that\n  \"fixes six arms\" would be overclaiming.*\n\n*** THE SHARPEST SINGLE FINDING, AND IT IS NOT MINE: THE MANUAL REQUIRES INITIALISING BOTH BASE\nREGISTERS TO ZERO (S6.4.14, `:10788`), AND `LCA_IOC_W_BASE1` REJECTS EXACTLY THAT. ***  A guest\nfollowing the manual own documented initialisation sequence terminates the emulator.  That is not a\ncorner value -- it is the prescribed setup.\n\n**Also established:** the registers are **64-bit, quadword access only** (S6.4 excludes longword,\n`:10175`); and **all six arms STORE THE VALUE BEFORE TESTING IT** (`d->window_mask_0 = idata;`\nprecedes the guard), so device state is already updated when the process dies.\n\n**DISPOSITION IS NOT SETTLED HERE.**  deepseek proposes accept-with-backing-storage for the values\nthe manual or existing code already treats as legal, plus a `(once)` decline otherwise, and warns\nagainst expanding the W_BASE set without evidence since the manual enumerates none. Counting references that are neither the readback (`odata = d->X`) nor the store (`d->X = `):\n\n    ioc_conf          1 consumer   <- `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` in lca_pci_conf.  THIS ROW.\n    tlb_enable        0\n    window_base_0     0            window_base_1     0\n    window_mask_0     0            window_mask_1     0\n    window_t_base_0   0            window_t_base_1   0\n\n**So the accepted-write-arms-a-later-kill shape cannot recur elsewhere in dev_lca.c**: the other\nseven fields are write-and-read-back with nothing downstream. And `ioc_conf` has **exactly two readers**, so nothing else can be armed by S1.\n\n*** FOUR CORRECTIONS THAT CHANGE THE CONSTRUCTION: ***\n\n1. **MY LIVENESS CONTROL IS MISLABELLED.**  `ioc rt` proves the WRITER (`lca_ioc`'s CONF arm)\n   round-trips `0x1`.  **It says nothing about S2's window.**  PCI_CONF liveness is the armed\n   session's unique message (the handler ran) PLUS the control surviving (the same access is not an\n   unconditional kill).  A positive signature is available if wanted: an empty-slot read returns\n   `0xffffffffffffffff`, where unmapped returns 0.\n2. **S2 MUST BE A READ, NOT A WRITE.**  Both reach the kill, but an empty-slot WRITE makes\n   `bus_pci_data_access` print `[ ... write to non-existant device ... ]` -- so the CONTROL would\n   carry a second `fatal()` on stderr.  An empty-slot READ of reg 0 is the architectural ID probe\n   and is silent.\n3. **A TIGHTER VARIANT: ONE PROCESS, not two sessions.**  control `ldq` (survives), then S1, then\n   the armed `ldq` (dies) -- **only `ioc_conf` changes between the two loads.**  Separate sessions\n   are still adequate, but this removes the residual that two processes could differ in something\n   that ran before the debugger prompt.\n4. **KEEP IT ON `step`.**  `continue` would make the `lca_interrupt_assert`/`_deassert` stubs a\n   confounder -- and `cpu_dyntrans.c` skips interrupt delivery while single-stepping anyway.\n\n*** THE CONTRACT LIMIT, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE FOR THE FIX ROUND: THIS IS A WITNESS OF\nTHE `exit(1)`, NOT A DETECTOR OF THE REPAIR. ***  grok: *\"'Host survives, session completes' greens\nfor a silent `return 1` that skips `bus_pci_*` and `memory_writemax64`, for a latched announce, AND\nfor falling through into existing `bus_pci` with `cur_bus=1` (empty type-1 also survives).\"*  So the\npost-fix oracle cannot discriminate between fix members, and a round that ships one must build a row\nthat can.  **Post-fix caution in the same breath: if someone later implements type-1 by falling\nthrough to `bus_pci_setaddr(bus=1, ...)`, empty bus 1 is a non-killing miss today, so the witness\ngoes green -- do NOT read that green as \"type-1 is modelled.\"**\n\n**WHY OFFSET 0 IS THE RIGHT CONTROL ADDRESS, which I had asserted without warrant:** it is not\nHAE-like (HAE is an exact-address `break` in `lca_ioc`; `lca_pci_conf` has no per-offset special\ncase).  On the ARMED session the offset is irrelevant -- `bus == 1` exits before `tag`/`dev` are\nused.  On the SURVIVING control, `tag == 0` matches no IDSEL bit, so the `dev >= 21` body runs --\n**and that body is already the commented-out kill with `dev = 0;`** -- then an empty-slot read\nreturns `(uint64_t)-1` without exiting.  `0x18` (the NetBSD `| 0x3 << 3` encoding) yields the same\n`tag`/`reg` and is equivalent.  **Do NOT use `relative_addr = 0x800000`**, which talks to the\nregistered `i82378zb` at device 7.\n\n*** AND grok FOUND THE DESIGN ARGUMENT SITTING FIVE LINES ABOVE THE DEFECT: the `dev >= 21` arm is\nALREADY DE-FATALISED, in the same function, and the surviving control USES that path. ***  *\"If that\n`exit(1)` were still live, the control would die and you would misread PCI_CONF as unconditionally\nfatal.  The file already contains the decline precedent the `else` lacks.\"*  That is the strongest\navailable warrant for declining here, and it is one function away from the kill.\n\nAlso confirmed: FAULT (member 5) is unavailable -- `memory_rw.c` delivers a failed device access as\nan exception only under `MEM_MIPS`/`MEM_M88K`, and this path always `return 1` on the live path, so\nthe kill is `exit(1)` and not `return 0`.  And `lca_isa`'s `ofs > len` kill is a DIFFERENT window\n(`LCA_PCI_SIO`), not reachable from `0x1e0000000`.\n\n*** CROSS-REFERENCE, AND A CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN NOVELTY CLAIM.  [[lcabadval]] ALREADY\nENUMERATED THIS KILL ON 2026-09-06. ***\n\nIts eleven-kill census for dev_lca.c lists **line 134, class BUS, condition `bus != 0`, i.e.\n`d->ioc_conf & 1`** -- so the site AND its dependence on ioc_conf were both already recorded, and I\nfiled this row saying only lcabadval mentioned lca_pci_conf without reading how.  **Sixth\nrediscovery this session; see [[refilingclass]], which predicts exactly this.**\n\n**WHAT THIS ROW GENUINELY ADDS, stated narrowly:** the census records the MECHANISM; it does not\nrecord that **`0x1` PASSES the CONF guard**.  That is what makes the kill reachable with no rejected\nvalue anywhere on the path -- an accepted write in one handler arming a terminating branch in\nanother -- and it is why the disposition differs from lcabadval's six value guards, whose kills all\nfire on refusal.  Plus the witness design, now reviewed SOUND by grok.\n\n**KEPT AS A SEPARATE ROW** because it has its own disposition (decline the config access, or\nimplement bus != 0) and its own witness, and because folding it into a row named for VALUE GUARDS\nwould hide it -- which is the census's own closing lesson: *a row named after one class hides the\nmembers of the others.*\n\n*** AND THE CENSUS NAMES SOMETHING PLAUSIBLY WORSE THAN THIS ROW, WHICH I SHOULD SAY OUT LOUD RATHER\nTHAN LEAVE IN A TABLE: the two INTERRUPT STUBS at :73 and :85. ***  `lca_interrupt_assert` and\n`lca_interrupt_deassert` are bare `fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1)` with **NO GUARD AT ALL**, so *any*\ndevice asserting an interrupt through the LCA ends the process regardless of what it writes.  In the\ncensus's words: the value-guard registers *\"need a firmware revision that writes something else;\nthese two need only that an interrupt is asserted.\"*  That is member 7 (IMPLEMENT) of the option\nset -- a whole path stubbed out, with no value being refused -- and it is a stronger candidate for\nthe next critical round than this one.\n\n*** THE RUNG-3 WITNESS EXISTS AND THE DEFECT IS MEASURED, 2026-09-07.  regress/lca_confbus_probe.py,\nartifact regress/artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/witness_HEAD_20260907.raw, sentinel LCACONFBUS_DONE. ***\n\n    bus ctl      yes                    yes                    CONTROL: identical read at ioc_conf==0 survived   ok\n    bus ctlsig   0xffffffffffffffff     0xffffffffffffffff     DEVICE SIGNATURE: empty-slot ID probe             ok\n    bus kill     dead                   alive                  <- THE DEFECT                                     FAIL\n    bus attrib   yes                    yes                    death carried THIS arm@s diagnostic               ok\n      EVIDENCE: TODO. Non-zero bus.\n    LCACONFBUS_RESULT=3/4\n\n**One emulator process, unmodified `alphabook1`, real guest instructions through real address\ndecode.**  The sequence is: guest `ldq` from `LCA_PCI_CONF+0` with `ioc_conf == 0` (SURVIVES), then\nguest `stq 0x1` to `LCA_IOC_CONF` (ACCEPTED -- `idata & ~1` is 0), then the IDENTICAL `ldq` again\n(HOST DIES).  Only `ioc_conf` differs between the two loads, which is what attributes the death to\nthe VALUE rather than to the address, the width, or touching the window at all.\n\n*** grok@s PREDICTED DEVICE SIGNATURE CAME BACK EXACTLY. ***  It reasoned from `bus_pci.c` that an\nempty-slot read returns `0xffffffffffffffff` via `memory_writemax64` while UNMAPPED returns 0.  The\ncontrol row reads **0xffffffffffffffff**.  That is a prediction from source, confirmed by\nmeasurement -- so the signature control is established, not assumed.\n\n**ORACLE POLARITY is the ladder@s: the rows assert the CORRECT (post-fix) behaviour, so `bus kill`\nis RED on the committed build and goes GREEN when the defect is fixed, with the SAME script.**\nClause (i) is therefore HALF discharged -- the pre-fix symptom is shown; the post-fix green awaits a\nfix, which is correct for test-first.\n\n*** TWO APPARATUS DEFECTS CAUGHT IN THE FIRST RUN AND FIXED, both worth keeping. ***\n1. **The first artifact carried NO EVIDENCE for `bus attrib`.**  The row tested the PTY buffer and\n   printed nothing, so the preserved log showed a green row whose diagnostic a later reader could\n   not check.  The probe now ECHOES the matched line into its own output.  *A green row whose\n   evidence is not in the artifact is not verifiable, however sound the logic.*\n2. **I nearly filed a false correction to grok.**  It said the `TODO. Non-zero bus.` literal is\n   unique in `src/`; my grep returned TWO.  The second hit is `dev_lca.o`, the compiled object.  One\n   source site, `dev_lca.c:133`.  grok was right; my apparatus swept binaries.\n\n*** THE FIX DESIGN IS CLEARER THAN THIS ROW ASSUMED, AND IT DIVERGES FROM ITS SIBLING FOR A REASON\nWORTH STATING.  Derived 2026-09-07 from the codex fix design on [[lcairqstubkill]] plus this row's\nown witness. ***\n\n**THE TWO LCA ROWS LOOK LIKE ONE PROBLEM AND ARE NOT.**  Both are bare `fatal()+exit(1)` reached by\nordinary guest access, so it is tempting to give them one disposition.  The difference is what the\nguest is ASKING FOR:\n\n* [[lcairqstubkill]] -- an interrupt DELIVERY.  There is no correct answer that is not delivery: a\n  decline LOSES AN EVENT, silently or otherwise, and the guest can never learn of it.  codex's\n  disposition there is \"diagnose and stop the guest CPU\", because continuing is a lie.\n* **THIS ROW -- a PCI configuration cycle on bus 1.  There IS a correct answer, and hardware gives\n  it: a config read of an absent device returns ALL ONES.**  An unimplemented BUS is legitimately\n  EMPTY.  Declining is not a lost event; it is the architecturally right reply.\n\n> **So the dispositions properly differ, and the reason is not \"one is worse\".  For a DELIVERY path\n> an unimplemented target has no honest continuation; for an ADDRESSING path it has one, and the\n> tree already implements it.**\n\n*** AND THE TREE ALREADY DOES THE RIGHT THING ONE STEP DOWN, which the witness measured by accident.\n***  The `bus == 0` branch already reaches `bus_pci_setaddr` / `bus_pci_data_access`, and this row's\nown witness recorded the result: **a read of an empty slot returns `0xffffffffffffffff`** (the\n`bus ctlsig` row, MEASURED).  grok noted the same about the armed side in its design review --\n*\"falling through into existing bus_pci with cur_bus=1 (empty type-1 also survives)\"*.\n\n**SO THE LIKELY FIX IS NOT A DECLINE AT ALL BUT A FALL-THROUGH:** stop special-casing `bus != 0`,\nlet the existing `bus_pci` path handle it, and an empty bus 1 answers all-ones by itself.  That is\noption-set member 7 (IMPLEMENT) reached cheaply, because the machinery exists -- unlike the sibling\nrow, where member 7 is blocked at the dyntrans core.\n\n*** THE DISCRIMINATING ROW IS THEREFORE THE RETURNED VALUE, NOT THE RUNNING STATE. ***  codex's\noracle for the sibling row (\"stopped guest state, plus the reason\") is right THERE and wrong HERE,\nbecause a correct fix must leave the guest RUNNING.  The observable that separates the candidates\nhere is what the armed read returns:\n\n    fall through to bus_pci (empty bus 1)      0xffffffffffffffff    <- architecturally correct\n    silent return without servicing            0x0 or unchanged      <- looks like UNMAPPED\n    latched announce then return unserviced    0x0 or unchanged      <- same, plus a message\n    the existing exit(1)                       host gone\n\n**And this probe ALREADY HAS THAT ROW SHAPE**: `bus ctlsig` asserts exactly `0xffffffffffffffff` on\nthe bus-0 control, and distinguishes it from the `0x0` an unmapped read gives.  The fix round adds\nthe mirror of it on the ARMED side.  *No new capability is needed, which is not true of the sibling.*\n\n**NOT YET VERIFIED, and it is the one thing to check before building:** that `bus_pci` genuinely\ntolerates `cur_bus == 1` rather than asserting or indexing something sized for one bus.  grok's\nreview says empty type-1 survives; **that is a seat's reading and this row has not measured it.**\nThe cheap check is to build the fall-through and run the existing probe -- the armed row should turn\nfrom `dead` to `0xffffffffffffffff`.\n\n*** THE UNVERIFIED LINK IS CLOSED BY READING: `bus_pci' IS BUS-AGNOSTIC BY CONSTRUCTION, SO THE\nFALL-THROUGH FIX IS SOUND WITHOUT A NEW CAPABILITY. ***\n\nThe paragraph above flagged one thing to check before building -- whether `bus_pci' tolerates\n`cur_bus == 1' rather than asserting or indexing something sized for a single bus.  It does:\n\n    bus_pci.h:84   int  cur_bus, cur_device, cur_func, cur_reg;   <- a plain int, not bus-0-only\n    bus_pci.h:87   struct pci_device *first_device;               <- a LINKED LIST, not an array\n                                                                     indexed by bus number\n    bus_pci.c:102  if (dev->bus == pci_data->cur_bus && ...)      <- devices carry their OWN bus\n                                                                     field and are MATCHED against it\n    bus_pci.c:200  pci_data->cur_bus = bus;                       <- set from the caller, unchecked\n\n**So a lookup with `cur_bus = 1` walks the list, matches nothing (no device carries `bus == 1`), and\nfalls into the no-device path -- which this row's own witness has already MEASURED returning\n`0xffffffffffffffff` on bus 0.**  Nothing is sized for one bus and nothing asserts.\n\n*** THAT CONFIRMS grok's READING AND UPGRADES IT FROM A SEAT CLAIM TO A CHECKED ONE. ***  Its design\nreview said *\"falling through into existing bus_pci with cur_bus=1 (empty type-1 also survives)\"*,\nwhich this row correctly recorded as unmeasured.  It is now verified by source rather than accepted.\n\n**CONSEQUENCE: the fix is a DELETION, not an addition.**  Remove the `bus != 0` special case and let\nthe existing path serve the cycle; an absent device on an absent bus answers all-ones by itself.\nThat is the cheapest member-7 in the option set -- and the addition licence is satisfied trivially,\nbecause nothing is added.\n\n**WHAT STILL OWES A MEASUREMENT, and it is one run:** build the fall-through and re-run\n`regress/lca_confbus_probe.py`.  **PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION: the `bus kill' row turns from `dead'\nto `alive', and a mirror of `bus ctlsig' on the armed side should read `0xffffffffffffffff`** -- the\nsame value the bus-0 control already returns, which is what distinguishes a serviced empty-bus cycle\nfrom a silent unserviced return (`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped).\n\n*** PASS-1 FIX PANEL, 2026-09-07 -- PROVISIONAL: recorded on THREE landed seats (deepseek, minimax,\nthe kimi-k3:cloud substitute) while codex, grok and glm were still answering.  Any ruling short of\nthe roster is provisional on its face. ***\n\n**Q1 IS SETTLED AND MY WORRY WAS UNFOUNDED -- AND THE PROOF IS INSIDE THE EXISTING CODE, WHICH I HAD\nNOT THOUGHT TO USE.**  I asked whether the file's tag comment describes the same `tag` this code\ncomputes (`(relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff`) or a pre-shift value.  minimax:\n\n> *\"The existing IDSEL check proves the code's `tag` IS the comment's `tag`.  The `bus == 0` branch\n> checks `tag & (0x800U << dev)` for dev 0..20.  The mask `1 << (dev+11)` only matches real hardware\n> if `tag` holds IDSEL bits at positions 11..31 -- exactly the comment's type-0 layout.  That branch\n> is EXERCISED TODAY on real bus-0 traffic and works, so the interpretation is FORCED.\"*\n\n`0x800` is exactly `1 << 11`, and the kimi substitute reaches it independently (*\"func at bits 10..8\nin both rows\"*).  **So `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is the faithful type-1 decode.**  *A working branch\nof the same function is a stronger oracle for a layout question than the comment is -- the comment\ncould be stale, the working code cannot be.*\n\n*** Q2 IS THE REAL DEFECT IN MY SKETCH, AND ALL THREE SEATS FLAG IT: THE FIX AS WRITTEN WOULD BE\nINCOHERENT. ***  deepseek: *\"the sketch is incomplete/incoherent because it keeps\n`bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` while the type-1 tag comment says bus is in tag bits 23..16.\"*  The kimi\nsubstitute sharpens it: **the model computes THREE bus/type sources from the address -- the AD[1:0]\ntype bits, the tag's bus field, and `ioc_conf & 1` -- and DISCARDS TWO.**  *\"The packet cannot say\nwhich one real hardware honours.\"*\n\n> **So decoding `dev` from the type-1 layout while taking `bus` from a register bit mixes two\n> addressing models.**  Either the tag is authoritative (and `bus` must come from bits 23..16) or the\n> register is (and the type-1 tag layout does not apply).  **The fix cannot be written until that is\n> chosen, and no seat could choose it from the packet.**\n\n**Q4: THE DISCRIMINATING ROW IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT -- unanimous among the three.**  My\nproposed armed-side all-ones row separates *serviced an empty bus* from *returned without servicing*\n(`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped).  **It cannot separate IMPLEMENT from an explicit all-ones\nreturn**, because an EMPTY bus 1 answers all-ones under both.  kimi: *\"only a populated bus-1 device\ncan\"* tell them apart -- and nothing in this tree places one.\n\n*That is a real limit on what this round can claim, and it is better to know it before building the\nrow than after.*\n\n**NOT ADOPTED PENDING THE ROSTER.**  The Q2 question is a hardware-behaviour question the packet\ncannot settle, and `_scratchpad/` holds no LCA/21066 datasheet (the `ls _scratchpad/*.pdf` list has\nDC21285 but no 21066), so it may not be settleable from this machine at all -- which would make it a\ncandidate for the 2026-09-09 adjudication rather than for a fix round.\n\n*** grok LANDED AND SETTLED Q2 -- THE ANSWER THE PACKET-FED SEATS COULD NOT REACH, AND IT DISSOLVES\nTHE INCOHERENCE RATHER THAN CHOOSING A SIDE. ***\n\n> **VERDICT: IMPLEMENT, but the sketch is one assignment short.**  `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is the\n> right extract of *this* `tag` (Q1 is not a double-shift).  **Also assign\n> `bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff` -- `ioc_conf & 1` is the PCI cycle TYPE, not the bus number.**\n> Member 7; fall through into `bus_pci`.  The proposed all-ones row is necessary and not sufficient:\n> the witness address is not a type-1 tag, and empty-slot data cannot pin the extract.\n\n**WHY THIS RESOLVES THE CONFLICT INSTEAD OF PICKING A WINNER.**  deepseek and the kimi substitute\nwere right that taking `dev` from the tag while taking `bus` from a register bit mixes two addressing\nmodels.  grok's reading shows the register bit is not a bus selector at all: it chooses the CYCLE\nTYPE -- type 0 addresses the local bus, type 1 addresses a bus behind a bridge -- and in type 1 the\nbus number comes from the tag.  **The file's own comment, \"the PCI bus 0 vs bus non-0 selection bit\",\nis a loose description of exactly that**, so the code comment and grok agree once the names are\nstraightened.  *The variable is simply misnamed: `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;` stores a cycle type in\nsomething called `bus`.*\n\n**THE FIX IS THEREFORE TWO ASSIGNMENTS, NOT ONE:**\n\n    } else {\n            bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;    /*  type-1: bus is bits 23..16  */\n            dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;    /*  type-1: device is bits 15..11  */\n    }\n\n*** AND grok BREAKS MY PROPOSED DISCRIMINATING ROW FOR A REASON I HAD NOT SEEN: \"the witness address\nis not a type-1 tag\". ***  The existing probe reads at `LCA_PCI_CONF + 0`, so `tag == 0` -- which\nunder the type-1 decode means bus 0, device 0.  **The row would therefore exercise the new code with\na degenerate tag and prove nothing about the extract**, and \"empty-slot data cannot pin the extract\"\nbecause an absent device answers all-ones whatever bus/dev were decoded.\n\n> **So the round needs a row that drives a NON-ZERO type-1 tag and observes something that depends\n> on the decoded bus/dev -- and with no device on bus 1, there is nothing to observe.**  That is the\n> same limit the three packet-fed seats reached from the other side (*\"only a populated bus-1 device\n> can\"* separate IMPLEMENT from a faked all-ones return).  Two independent routes to one conclusion.\n\n**HARDWARE CLAIM, FLAGGED AS UNVERIFIABLE FROM THIS MACHINE:** that `ioc_conf & 1` is the cycle type\nis a statement about the 21066/LCA, and **`_scratchpad/` holds no LCA datasheet** -- the PDF list has\nDC21285 but nothing for this chipset.  It is consistent with the file's comment and with standard PCI\ntype-0/type-1 addressing, but it is not CHECKED here, and this project's rule is to cite only what\nhas been read.  **That is a good candidate for the 2026-09-09 adjudication rather than for me.**\n\n*** THE PANEL IS COMPLETE (6 seats) AND UNANIMOUS ON THE DESIGN -- AND codex CITED A PRIMARY SOURCE\nSITTING ON THIS MACHINE THAT I HAD JUST DECLARED UNAVAILABLE. ***\n\nI wrote, one paragraph above, that the cycle-type claim is *\"a statement about the 21066 ... not\nCHECKED here\"* because `_scratchpad/` holds no LCA datasheet.  **codex pointed at\n`_guest-src-openbsd22/sys/arch/alpha/pci/lca_pci.c` -- the REAL OpenBSD LCA driver, 4,925 bytes,\ndated 1997, on disk in this project.**  Verified and read:\n\n    :103   if (bp != NULL)  *bp = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;      /*  BUS      */\n    :106   if (dp != NULL)  *dp = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;      /*  DEVICE   */\n    :108   if (fp != NULL)  *fp = (tag >>  8) & 0x7;       /*  FUNCTION */\n\n**That is grok's proposal, verbatim, from the driver the hardware was written for.**  `bus` is bits\n23..16 of the tag and `dev` is bits 15..11 -- not inference, not a datasheet paraphrase, the actual\ndecomposition routine.\n\n*** AND `lca_conf_read` SETTLES Q2 OUTRIGHT: ***\n\n    :125   /* secondary if bus # != 0 */\n    :126   pci_decompose_tag(&lcp->lc_pc, tag, &secondary, &device, 0);\n    :127   if (secondary) {\n    :130           REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01;\n    :132   } else {\n    :134           /*  on the LCA, must frob the tag used for devices on the primary bus,\n    :136              in the same ways as is used by type 1 configuration cycles on PCs.  */\n    :139           tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (tag & 0x7ff);\n    :140   }\n\n**The driver derives the bus from the TAG and then writes `IOC_CONF = 0x01` to select the SECONDARY\nconfiguration FORMAT.**  So `ioc_conf & 1` is the cycle type, exactly as grok and codex said and as\nthe fork's own loosely-worded comment (\"bus 0 vs bus non-0 selection bit\") was groping at.  **The\nfork's variable is misnamed**, and its type-0 handling is correct -- `(1 << (device + 11))` is\nprecisely the IDSEL form the `0x800U << dev` loop scans for.\n\n*** THE ROW IS MASSIVELY STRENGTHENED, AND THIS IS THE PART THAT MATTERS. ***  My witness writes\n`0x1` to `LCA_IOC_CONF` and then performs a config access, and I described that as an ordinary but\nsynthetic pair of guest accesses.  **It is not synthetic: it is the OpenBSD driver's own\nsecondary-bus path, line for line.**  `REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01` followed by the config read is\nwhat `lca_conf_read` DOES whenever it is asked about a device behind a bridge.\n\n> **So the kill is not merely reachable in principle -- it is on the code path a real guest driver\n> takes the moment it probes a secondary bus.**  The row previously said \"NOT CLAIMED: that any\n> shipped guest issues this sequence.\"  That caution can now be withdrawn: a shipped guest driver\n> issues exactly this sequence, by construction, for secondary-bus config cycles.\n\n**THE FIX, now backed by the driver rather than by reading the fork's comment:**\n\n    } else {\n            bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;    /*  type-1: bus is bits 23..16   */\n            dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;    /*  type-1: device is bits 15..11 */\n    }\n\n**WHAT IS STILL NOT SETTLED, and every seat agrees:** the discriminating row.  grok -- *\"the witness\naddress is not a type-1 tag, and empty-slot data cannot pin the extract\"*; glm -- the row *\"conflates\nIMPLEMENT with explicit-all-ones DECLINE, silent with latched, and can false-pass on stale all-ones\nunless the destination register is pre-seeded\"*; the kimi substitute and minimax -- only a POPULATED\nbus-1 device could separate them.  **Four independent routes to one wall.**  glm's pre-seeding point\nis the one actionable improvement: seed the destination register so a stale all-ones cannot pass.\n\n*The lesson about this machine is the `ls _scratchpad/*.pdf` lesson again, one directory over: I\ndeclared a fact unverifiable while its primary source sat in the project.  **Before calling a\nhardware question unanswerable here, look for the guest OS source tree.***\n\n*** A FACT THIS ROW'S QUESTION DOES NOT CARRY, ESTABLISHED 2026-09-08 FROM THREE INDEPENDENT FILES:\nOPTION-SET MEMBER 5 (FAULT) IS NOT AVAILABLE ON ALPHA. ***\n\nThis is an Alpha machine, and the adjudication of this row weighs dispositions from the standing\noption set.  **FAULT looks available and is not**, which is the option-set-completeness problem\ninverted -- not a missing member, a PHANTOM one.\n\n    memory_rw.c        the device-failure dispatcher has MIPS and M88K
