VERDICT: Support member 3 with `ofs >= len` and failure checked before packing, as a bounded repair to the existing byte-lane contract. Correct the producer domain and detector channels before approving the design: the sixteen cells are incomplete, and guest register preservation cannot detect packing before a failed-read return.

I read `C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/`. The enclosing function matches the packet, and the supplied three commits match local history. I ran no builds, probes, gates, or mutations and changed no files.

For 1(a)–(c), the byte-lane decode is correct, but the description of transfer size needs correction.

Walking the requested address:

| Step | Value |
|---|---|
| Sparse offset `0x3fd << 5` | `0x7fa0` |
| `a<6:5>` | `01` |
| `a<4:3>` | `00` |
| `a<2:0>` | `000` |
| Model `relative_addr >> 5` | Port `0x3fd` |
| Model `ofs = port & 3` | `1` |
| Hardware PCI address upper portion | `0x3fc` |
| Hardware PCI I/O `ad<1:0>` | `01` |
| Resulting PCI I/O address | `0x3fd` |

Thus an LDL byte read of the LSR has the right lane placement: with device byte `0x60`, the buffer is `[0x00, 0x60, 0x00, 0x00]`, the register receives `0x6000`, and the driver extracts `0x60`.

That establishes packing correctness **provided the downstream call reaches the intended device**. The missing `PHYSICAL` flag prevents an unconditional claim about operation after virtual memory is configured.

The model does discard `a<4:3>`, but **it does not use `len` as the downstream transfer size**. Both calls explicitly transfer **one byte**. `len` controls the guard, write-byte selection, and read-buffer packing. See [dev_lca.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c), grep `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)`.

Here is the complete encoding walk. Entries give the documented transfer and active-low byte enables:

| `a<4:3>` | `a<6:5>=00` | `01` | `10` | `11` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `00` | Byte / `1110` | Byte / `1101` | Byte / `1011` | Byte / `0111` |
| `01` | Word / `1100` | Word / `1001` | Word / `0011` | Unspecified |
| `10` | Tribyte / `1000` | Tribyte / `0001` | Unspecified | Unspecified |
| `11` | Longword / `0000` | Unspecified | Unspecified | Quadword / `0000` |

A **word at lane 3 is not a documented legal encoding**. The legal word, tribyte, longword, and quadword transfers are incompletely implemented by forwarding one byte. I support a separate `lcaisasize` row for that defect. Also, the manual lists LDL/STL for masked transfers; “longword or quadword whatever the encoded size” is too broad. The packet’s byte-enable data is staggered too: lane-1 byte enable is `1101`. [21066/21066A HRM, grep “Table 6–3 Byte Enable Encoding” and “Masked Transfers”](https://openwatcom.org/ftp/devel/docs/21066_66a_hrm.pdf).

S4 is confirmed independently: [cpu_alpha_instr.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr.c), grep `case 0x0a:`, `case 0x0c:`, `case 0x0d:`, and `case 0x0e:`, installs LDBU, LDWU, STW, and STB handlers without a CPU-type gate. These are guest instruction paths. A load targeting `ALPHA_ZERO` is explicitly suppressed, so detector loads must use an ordinary destination register.

For 1(d), **a declined ordinary Alpha load preserves its destination register and execution continues**.

The source chain is:

- [memory_rw.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c), grep `mem->devices[i].f(` and `if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions)`: failed device access returns `MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED`; the exception calls there are guarded by `MEM_MIPS` and `MEM_M88K`.
- [cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c), grep `LS_GENERIC_N`: failed `memory_rw` returns before register assignment.
- [cpu_dyntrans.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c), grep `next_ic ++; ic->f(cpu, ic);`: ordinary instruction dispatch has already advanced.

Zeroing the caller’s read buffer therefore does **not** load zero on failure. Use a nonzero destination sentinel distinct from the successful answer, plus a subsequent execution marker.

For an ordinary store, the source register remains unchanged on success too. **Register preservation is a vacuous store-decline channel.** Observe downstream calls/state and, at the handler/API boundary, failure status.

For 1(e), first audit the producer. The Alpha template supplies buffers of lengths `1`, `2`, `4`, and `8`. Sparse offsets `0`, `32`, `64`, and `96` produce lanes `0`, `1`, `2`, and `3`, respectively; each offset satisfies alignment for all four widths. Thus all sixteen **unsplit** cells are reachable.

However, ordinary misalignment falls back to `LS_GENERIC_N`; it is not rejected. `memory_rw` then splits translated accesses using `MRW_SPLIT_MASK = 0x3ff`. Alpha translation preserves those low address bits. S5’s unconditional length census is consequently false.

For the requested sixteen cells, let `B[j]` mean a buffer with the successful downstream byte at index `j` and zeros elsewhere. “Served” below means correct under the retained **single-byte packing convention**, not complete hardware transfer emulation.

| `(len, ofs)` | Today: read | Today: write selects | Candidate | Detector row |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `(1,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A10 |
| `(1,1)` | False success: zeros | `data[0]`, wrapped | Declined | D11 |
| `(1,2)` | Exit | No selection | Declined | D12 |
| `(1,3)` | Exit | No selection | Declined | D13 |
| `(2,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A20 |
| `(2,1)` | Served: `B[1]` | `data[1]` | Still served | A21 |
| `(2,2)` | False success: zeros | `data[0]`, wrapped | Declined | D22 |
| `(2,3)` | Exit | No selection | Declined | D23 |
| `(4,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A40 |
| `(4,1)` | Served: `B[1]` | `data[1]` | Still served | A41 |
| `(4,2)` | Served: `B[2]` | `data[2]` | Still served | A42 |
| `(4,3)` | Served: `B[3]` | `data[3]` | Still served | A43 |
| `(8,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A80 |
| `(8,1)` | Served: `B[1]` | `data[1]` | Still served | A81 |
| `(8,2)` | Served: `B[2]` | `data[2]` | Still served | A82 |
| `(8,3)` | Served: `B[3]` | `data[3]` | Still served | A83 |

The `(2,3)` premise is wrong: **the write never reaches `3 % 2`**. The guard exits first. Were the guard bypassed, selecting `data[1]` would still transfer only one byte, not implement a word or tribyte. Also, the expression reads the caller’s buffer; it does not assign into it.

At equality, the read never evaluates the ternary’s `byte` operand. Its false-success defect is distinct from failed-byte consumption. Equality writes really forward `data[0]`; calling those hardware-correct would require semantics for the unsupported narrow instruction/container combination.

Here is the split producer’s boundary walk. `r` is the starting offset modulo 1024. Every listed head has `(r >> 5) & 3 == 3`; every tail starts at lane zero. Tail reachability assumes the preceding downstream operation succeeds.

| Instruction width | `r` | Head `(len,ofs)` | Tail `(len,ofs)` | Tail reached: today / candidate |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| 2 | 1023 | `(1,3)` | `(1,0)` | No / No |
| 4 | 1021 | `(3,3)` | `(1,0)` | Yes / No |
| 4 | 1022 | `(2,3)` | `(2,0)` | No / No |
| 4 | 1023 | `(1,3)` | `(3,0)` | No / No |
| 8 | 1017 | `(7,3)` | `(1,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1018 | `(6,3)` | `(2,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1019 | `(5,3)` | `(3,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1020 | `(4,3)` | `(4,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1021 | `(3,3)` | `(5,0)` | Yes / No |
| 8 | 1022 | `(2,3)` | `(6,0)` | No / No |
| 8 | 1023 | `(1,3)` | `(7,0)` | No / No |

Ingress from a **successfully accessed preceding region** also supplies `(5,0)`, `(6,0)`, and `(7,0)`: an eight-byte instruction starts three, two, or one bytes before the window, respectively. This matters because the candidate blocks the internal route to `(5,0)` shown above.

The additional cells are:

| `(len,ofs)` | Today | Candidate | Detector row |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(3,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A30 |
| `(3,3)` | False-success read zeros; write `data[0]` | Declined | D33 |
| `(5,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A50 |
| `(5,3)` | Read `B[3]`; write `data[3]` | Still served | A53 |
| `(6,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A60 |
| `(6,3)` | Read `B[3]`; write `data[3]` | Still served | A63 |
| `(7,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A70 |
| `(7,3)` | Read `B[3]`; write `data[3]` | Still served | A73 |

These are chunk-buffer results. A split instruction writes its destination only if its complete memory operation succeeds. I have not established an exhaustive length census for every non-instruction caller of the memory API.

For question 2, your listed mutations are meaningful, with the channel qualifications below. In particular, changing `>=` back to `>` leaves D12 green; testing only an originally terminating cell misses equality.

Additional small mutations and specific rows expected to remain green:

| Mutation | Row that stays green | Required distinguishing observation |
|---|---|---|
| Widen refusal to `ofs >= len - 1` | D12 | Supported boundary cells, including A10, A21, A43 |
| Change read placement to `i == 0` | A10 / existing `live` | Full packed result at nonzero lanes |
| Change write selection to `data[0]` | A41’s read check / `ofs1 wide` | Distinct input bytes on supported writes |
| Clear the buffer for writes too | All accept-read checks | Forwarded write byte and unchanged input buffer |
| Clear port low bits before forwarding | A40 | Correct downstream address at nonzero lanes |
| Replace write-result propagation with successful completion | Read-failure row | Write API failure status |
| Replace warning-mask `|=` with `=` | Repetition without other warnings | Interleaved IOC/ISA warnings must preserve each other |
| Use a function-static latch | Single-instance repetition | Independent first announcement from another instance |

A test backend returning the same byte for every address cannot detect misrouting solely through readback; accept rows should check the forwarded address too.

For question 3, I see no necessary eighth local disposition. The seven labels are not mutually exclusive, however: an announced successful fallback combines an announcement policy with an acceptance policy.

**Equality can be made bounds-safe and successfully serviced**, for example by defining a consistent narrow-access packing rule matching the wrapped write selection. But that is a newly chosen emulation contract. Placing the byte somewhere in bounds does not establish real-21066 correctness, because these narrow CPU accesses lack a documented hardware contract here. Thus member 4 is available as an explicit compatibility choice; the packet does not prove it mandatory or uniquely correct.

The sibling idiom also needs precise attribution:

- `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)`, grep `default:` and `memory_writemax64`: its default returns **1**, answering reads with zero and discarding writes.
- `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`, grep `if (type1 && bus == 0)`: it constructs an empty-slot answer and returns **1**.

They support bounded announcements and defined fallback responses. **Neither establishes a house requirement to return 0.**

Member 7 is the appropriate eventual disposition for `lcaisasize`. It requires retaining the raw encoded size, validating legal combinations, transferring every enabled byte, packing the result, and defining device-boundary and partial-failure behavior. Merely forwarding `len` bytes would still be wrong.

The prose sketch I support for this round is: obtain the instance from `extra`; clear reads only; preserve the port/lane decode; refuse `ofs >= len` before forwarding; announce through a fresh instance bit; put the refusal return outside the latch condition; retain immediate write-result propagation using `data[ofs]`; check read failure before packing; and let only successful reads reach the final success return. Physical forwarding should use `CACHE_NONE | PHYSICAL` in both directions, with dedicated validation. Print `ofs` with `%u` and `len` with `%zu`.

For question 4, the A/D rows in the two cell tables are the per-cell detector rows. Each needs read and write checks:

| Row family | Required oracle |
|---|---|
| Every A row | Successful completion; exactly one downstream byte operation; correct address/direction; complete `B[ofs]` read buffer; selected `data[ofs]` write byte; input buffer preserved |
| Every D row | Failure return in both directions; zero downstream calls; zero read buffer; ordinary guest load preserves a distinct sentinel and reaches its next marker; repeated refusals produce one attributable announcement per instance |
| A43 and A83 | Include a byte with its high bit set: LDL sign-extends its packed longword; LDQ does not |
| A50, A60, A70 | Produce through successful ingress or a handler fixture; do not place an earlier refused chunk in the path |

The remaining rows should be:

| Row | Required oracle |
|---|---|
| Exit reinstatement | Restoring the terminating disposition makes completion fail on an originally terminating cell; attribution alone is insufficient |
| Hardware-byte LSR preservation | The supplied LDL byte encoding still produces `0x6000`, with extracted LSR `0x60` |
| Failed read: status | Backend fails while CPU remains running; handler returns 0; integrated load preserves a nonzero sentinel |
| Failed read: ordering | Backend supplies a distinctive nonzero byte and returns failure; inspect the handler’s output buffer and require zero |
| Failed write: status | Backend fails while CPU remains running; handler/API returns 0 |
| Latch repetition | Repeated refusals, including mixed read/write directions, produce one site announcement |
| Latch independence | Interleave IOC → ISA → IOC and ISA → IOC → ISA; neither reason suppresses or re-enables the other |
| Instance independence | Two fresh LCA instances each announce their first refusal |
| Physical read forwarding | Under nonidentity translation, reach the intended physical backend and return its byte |
| Physical write forwarding | Under nonidentity translation, modify the intended physical backend |

**The failed-read ordering row is vacuous if it observes only the guest register.** Moving the failure check below packing still returns 0, so Alpha still skips writeback. Observe the handler buffer with a distinctive failed byte. A failure source that always supplies zero also masks that particular ordering mutation.

Other channel traps are:

- A CPU-stopping failure masks lost handler status because the outer dispatcher detects the running-to-stopped transition.
- An unchanged ordinary store source register proves nothing about decline.
- An unchanged read-only device register does not prove zero downstream calls.
- Bootstrap identity translation cannot distinguish retaining versus dropping `PHYSICAL`.
- The witness’s `attrib` rows explicitly accept any surviving run; they are not repair detectors.
- `NO_EXCEPTIONS` access to this `DM_DEFAULT` device skips its handler in the dispatcher, so such an access cannot certify these rows.

Instrumented call counts and buffer checks establish handler properties; they do not become guest-execution witnesses merely by sharing a detector.

For question 5, these additional premise corrections remain:

- S1 is confirmed: [debugmsg.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c), grep `void fatal(` and `static void va_debug(`, prints and returns. The current working-tree `lca_isa` contains `dev_lca.c`’s sole executable process exit.
- The local [ledger](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/pipeline/ledger.json), grep `"id": "lcaisalane"`, says `sev: med`, `state: open`, `opened: 2026-09-07`, and `witness: null`. That disagrees with the packet. Both supplied witness logs do contain `13/16` and `LCAISALANE_DONE`.
- There are **six IOC latch bits**, not eight: `CONF_CFG1=2`, `WMASK0=3`, `WMASK1=4`, `WEN=5`, `DEF_W=6`, `DEF_R=7`. The other occupied bits are `IRQ_ASSERT=0` and `IRQ_DEASSERT=1`. Bit 8 is currently free. Testing independence against one IOC bit establishes only that collision case; enumerate all six if claiming complete IOC independence.
- The uninitialized-byte claim remains conditional. Existing translation-failure and device-dispatch paths initialize read storage; the nonexistent-memory halt path can return earlier. This does not establish guest disclosure.
- Quadword I/O addressing needs a separate resolution: Table 6-3 prints I/O `ad<1:0>=00` for the quadword encoding, while the manual’s “I/O Space Cycles” prose says those bits copy `a<6:5>`. I cannot settle that inconsistency from this material. It does not affect the byte-address walk or the established missing multi-byte transfer behavior.

A **word at lane 3 is not a documented legal encoding**. The legal word, tribyte, longword, and quadword transfers are incompletely implemented by forwarding one byte. I support a separate `lcaisasize` row for that defect. Also, the manual lists LDL/STL for masked transfers; “longword or quadword whatever the encoded size” is too broad. The packet’s byte-enable data is staggered too: lane-1 byte enable is `1101`. [21066/21066A HRM, grep “Table 6–3 Byte Enable Encoding” and “Masked Transfers”](https://openwatcom.org/ftp/devel/docs/21066_66a_hrm.pdf).

S4 is confirmed independently: [cpu_alpha_instr.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr.c), grep `case 0x0a:`, `case 0x0c:`, `case 0x0d:`, and `case 0x0e:`, installs LDBU, LDWU, STW, and STB handlers without a CPU-type gate. These are guest instruction paths. A load targeting `ALPHA_ZERO` is explicitly suppressed, so detector loads must use an ordinary destination register.

For 1(d), **a declined ordinary Alpha load preserves its destination register and execution continues**.

The source chain is:

- [memory_rw.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c), grep `mem->devices[i].f(` and `if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions)`: failed device access returns `MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED`; the exception calls there are guarded by `MEM_MIPS` and `MEM_M88K`.
- [cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c), grep `LS_GENERIC_N`: failed `memory_rw` returns before register assignment.
- [cpu_dyntrans.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c), grep `next_ic ++; ic->f(cpu, ic);`: ordinary instruction dispatch has already advanced.

Zeroing the caller’s read buffer therefore does **not** load zero on failure. Use a nonzero destination sentinel distinct from the successful answer, plus a subsequent execution marker.

For an ordinary store, the source register remains unchanged on success too. **Register preservation is a vacuous store-decline channel.** Observe downstream calls/state and, at the handler/API boundary, failure status.

For 1(e), first audit the producer. The Alpha template supplies buffers of lengths `1`, `2`, `4`, and `8`. Sparse offsets `0`, `32`, `64`, and `96` produce lanes `0`, `1`, `2`, and `3`, respectively; each offset satisfies alignment for all four widths. Thus all sixteen **unsplit** cells are reachable.

However, ordinary misalignment falls back to `LS_GENERIC_N`; it is not rejected. `memory_rw` then splits translated accesses using `MRW_SPLIT_MASK = 0x3ff`. Alpha translation preserves those low address bits. S5’s unconditional length census is consequently false.

For the requested sixteen cells, let `B[j]` mean a buffer with the successful downstream byte at index `j` and zeros elsewhere. “Served” below means correct under the retained **single-byte packing convention**, not complete hardware transfer emulation.

| `(len, ofs)` | Today: read | Today: write selects | Candidate | Detector row |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `(1,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A10 |
| `(1,1)` | False success: zeros | `data[0]`, wrapped | Declined | D11 |
| `(1,2)` | Exit | No selection | Declined | D12 |
| `(1,3)` | Exit | No selection | Declined | D13 |
| `(2,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A20 |
| `(2,1)` | Served: `B[1]` | `data[1]` | Still served | A21 |
| `(2,2)` | False success: zeros | `data[0]`, wrapped | Declined | D22 |
| `(2,3)` | Exit | No selection | Declined | D23 |
| `(4,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A40 |
| `(4,1)` | Served: `B[1]` | `data[1]` | Still served | A41 |
| `(4,2)` | Served: `B[2]` | `data[2]` | Still served | A42 |
| `(4,3)` | Served: `B[3]` | `data[3]` | Still served | A43 |
| `(8,0)` | Served: `B[0]` | `data[0]` | Still served | A80 |
| `(8,1)` | Served: `B[1]` | `data[1]` | Still served | A81 |
| `(8,2)` | Served: `B[2]` | `data[2]` | Still served | A82 |
| `(8,3)` | Served: `B[3]` | `data[3]` | Still served | A83 |

The `(2,3)` premise is wrong: **the write never reaches `3 % 2`**. The guard exits first. Were the guard bypassed, selecting `data[1]` would still transfer only one byte, not implement a word or tribyte. Also, the expression reads the caller’s buffer; it does not assign into it.

At equality, the read never evaluates the ternary’s `byte` operand. Its false-success defect is distinct from failed-byte consumption. Equality writes really forward `data[0]`; calling those hardware-correct would require semantics for the unsupported narrow instruction/container combination.

Here is the split producer’s boundary walk. `r` is the starting offset modulo 1024. Every listed head has `(r >> 5) & 3 == 3`; every tail starts at lane zero. Tail reachability assumes the preceding downstream operation succeeds.

| Instruction width | `r` | Head `(len,ofs)` | Tail `(len,ofs)` | Tail reached: today / candidate |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| 2 | 1023 | `(1,3)` | `(1,0)` | No / No |
| 4 | 1021 | `(3,3)` | `(1,0)` | Yes / No |
| 4 | 1022 | `(2,3)` | `(2,0)` | No / No |
| 4 | 1023 | `(1,3)` | `(3,0)` | No / No |
| 8 | 1017 | `(7,3)` | `(1,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1018 | `(6,3)` | `(2,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1019 | `(5,3)` | `(3,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1020 | `(4,3)` | `(4,0)` | Yes / Yes |
| 8 | 1021 | `(3,3)` | `(5,0)` | Yes / No |
| 8 | 1022 | `(2,3)` | `(6,0)` | No / No |
| 8 | 1023 | `(1,3)` | `(7,0)` | No / No |

Ingress from a **successfully accessed preceding region** also supplies `(5,0)`, `(6,0)`, and `(7,0)`: an eight-byte instruction starts three, two, or one bytes before the window, respectively. This matters because the candidate blocks the internal route to `(5,0)` shown above.

The additional cells are:

| `(len,ofs)` | Today | Candidate | Detector row |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(3,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A30 |
| `(3,3)` | False-success read zeros; write `data[0]` | Declined | D33 |
| `(5,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A50 |
| `(5,3)` | Read `B[3]`; write `data[3]` | Still served | A53 |
| `(6,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A60 |
| `(6,3)` | Read `B[3]`; write `data[3]` | Still served | A63 |
| `(7,0)` | Read `B[0]`; write `data[0]` | Still served | A70 |
| `(7,3)` | Read `B[3]`; write `data[3]` | Still served | A73 |

These are chunk-buffer results. A split instruction writes its destination only if its complete memory operation succeeds. I have not established an exhaustive length census for every non-instruction caller of the memory API.

For question 2, your listed mutations are meaningful, with the channel qualifications below. In particular, changing `>=` back to `>` leaves D12 green; testing only an originally terminating cell misses equality.

Additional small mutations and specific rows expected to remain green:

| Mutation | Row that stays green | Required distinguishing observation |
|---|---|---|
| Widen refusal to `ofs >= len - 1` | D12 | Supported boundary cells, including A10, A21, A43 |
| Change read placement to `i == 0` | A10 / existing `live` | Full packed result at nonzero lanes |
| Change write selection to `data[0]` | A41’s read check / `ofs1 wide` | Distinct input bytes on supported writes |
| Clear the buffer for writes too | All accept-read checks | Forwarded write byte and unchanged input buffer |
| Clear port low bits before forwarding | A40 | Correct downstream address at nonzero lanes |
| Replace write-result propagation with successful completion | Read-failure row | Write API failure status |
| Replace warning-mask `|=` with `=` | Repetition without other warnings | Interleaved IOC/ISA warnings must preserve each other |
| Use a function-static latch | Single-instance repetition | Independent first announcement from another instance |

A test backend returning the same byte for every address cannot detect misrouting solely through readback; accept rows should check the forwarded address too.

For question 3, I see no necessary eighth local disposition. The seven labels are not mutually exclusive, however: an announced successful fallback combines an announcement policy with an acceptance policy.

**Equality can be made bounds-safe and successfully serviced**, for example by defining a consistent narrow-access packing rule matching the wrapped write selection. But that is a newly chosen emulation contract. Placing the byte somewhere in bounds does not establish real-21066 correctness, because these narrow CPU accesses lack a documented hardware contract here. Thus member 4 is available as an explicit compatibility choice; the packet does not prove it mandatory or uniquely correct.

The sibling idiom also needs precise attribution:

- `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)`, grep `default:` and `memory_writemax64`: its default returns **1**, answering reads with zero and discarding writes.
- `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`, grep `if (type1 && bus == 0)`: it constructs an empty-slot answer and returns **1**.

They support bounded announcements and defined fallback responses. **Neither establishes a house requirement to return 0.**

Member 7 is the appropriate eventual disposition for `lcaisasize`. It requires retaining the raw encoded size, validating legal combinations, transferring every enabled byte, packing the result, and defining device-boundary and partial-failure behavior. Merely forwarding `len` bytes would still be wrong.

The prose sketch I support for this round is: obtain the instance from `extra`; clear reads only; preserve the port/lane decode; refuse `ofs >= len` before forwarding; announce through a fresh instance bit; put the refusal return outside the latch condition; retain immediate write-result propagation using `data[ofs]`; check read failure before packing; and let only successful reads reach the final success return. Physical forwarding should use `CACHE_NONE | PHYSICAL` in both directions, with dedicated validation. Print `ofs` with `%u` and `len` with `%zu`.

For question 4, the A/D rows in the two cell tables are the per-cell detector rows. Each needs read and write checks:

| Row family | Required oracle |
|---|---|
| Every A row | Successful completion; exactly one downstream byte operation; correct address/direction; complete `B[ofs]` read buffer; selected `data[ofs]` write byte; input buffer preserved |
| Every D row | Failure return in both directions; zero downstream calls; zero read buffer; ordinary guest load preserves a distinct sentinel and reaches its next marker; repeated refusals produce one attributable announcement per instance |
| A43 and A83 | Include a byte with its high bit set: LDL sign-extends its packed longword; LDQ does not |
| A50, A60, A70 | Produce through successful ingress or a handler fixture; do not place an earlier refused chunk in the path |

The remaining rows should be:

| Row | Required oracle |
|---|---|
| Exit reinstatement | Restoring the terminating disposition makes completion fail on an originally terminating cell; attribution alone is insufficient |
| Hardware-byte LSR preservation | The supplied LDL byte encoding still produces `0x6000`, with extracted LSR `0x60` |
| Failed read: status | Backend fails while CPU remains running; handler returns 0; integrated load preserves a nonzero sentinel |
| Failed read: ordering | Backend supplies a distinctive nonzero byte and returns failure; inspect the handler’s output buffer and require zero |
| Failed write: status | Backend fails while CPU remains running; handler/API returns 0 |
| Latch repetition | Repeated refusals, including mixed read/write directions, produce one site announcement |
| Latch independence | Interleave IOC → ISA → IOC and ISA → IOC → ISA; neither reason suppresses or re-enables the other |
| Instance independence | Two fresh LCA instances each announce their first refusal |
| Physical read forwarding | Under nonidentity translation, reach the intended physical backend and return its byte |
| Physical write forwarding | Under nonidentity translation, modify the intended physical backend |

**The failed-read ordering row is vacuous if it observes only the guest register.** Moving the failure check below packing still returns 0, so Alpha still skips writeback. Observe the handler buffer with a distinctive failed byte. A failure source that always supplies zero also masks that particular ordering mutation.

Other channel traps are:

- A CPU-stopping failure masks lost handler status because the outer dispatcher detects the running-to-stopped transition.
- An unchanged ordinary store source register proves nothing about decline.
- An unchanged read-only device register does not prove zero downstream calls.
- Bootstrap identity translation cannot distinguish retaining versus dropping `PHYSICAL`.
- The witness’s `attrib` rows explicitly accept any surviving run; they are not repair detectors.
- `NO_EXCEPTIONS` access to this `DM_DEFAULT` device skips its handler in the dispatcher, so such an access cannot certify these rows.

Instrumented call counts and buffer checks establish handler properties; they do not become guest-execution witnesses merely by sharing a detector.

For question 5, these additional premise corrections remain:

- S1 is confirmed: [debugmsg.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c), grep `void fatal(` and `static void va_debug(`, prints and returns. The current working-tree `lca_isa` contains `dev_lca.c`’s sole executable process exit.
- The local [ledger](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/pipeline/ledger.json), grep `"id": "lcaisalane"`, says `sev: med`, `state: open`, `opened: 2026-09-07`, and `witness: null`. That disagrees with the packet. Both supplied witness logs do contain `13/16` and `LCAISALANE_DONE`.
- There are **six IOC latch bits**, not eight: `CONF_CFG1=2`, `WMASK0=3`, `WMASK1=4`, `WEN=5`, `DEF_W=6`, `DEF_R=7`. The other occupied bits are `IRQ_ASSERT=0` and `IRQ_DEASSERT=1`. Bit 8 is currently free. Testing independence against one IOC bit establishes only that collision case; enumerate all six if claiming complete IOC independence.
- The uninitialized-byte claim remains conditional. Existing translation-failure and device-dispatch paths initialize read storage; the nonexistent-memory halt path can return earlier. This does not establish guest disclosure.
- Quadword I/O addressing needs a separate resolution: Table 6-3 prints I/O `ad<1:0>=00` for the quadword encoding, while the manual’s “I/O Space Cycles” prose says those bits copy `a<6:5>`. I cannot settle that inconsistency from this material. It does not affect the byte-address walk or the established missing multi-byte transfer behavior.
